The intrinsic cohomology ring of the universal compactified Jacobian over the moduli space of stable curves
Younghan Bae, Davesh Maulik, Junliang Shen, Qizheng Yin

Abstract
. The purpose of this paper is to study the cohomology rings of universal compactified Jacobians. Over the moduli space ℳ g {\mathcal{M}}_{g} of nonsingular curves, we show that the cohomology ring of the universal Jacobian is independent of the degree. Over the moduli space ℳ ¯ g , n \overline{{\mathcal{M}}}_{g,n} of Deligne–Mumford stable marked curves with n ≥ 1 n\geq 1 , on the one hand we show that the cohomology ring of a universal fine compactified Jacobian is sensitive to the choice of a nondegenerate stability condition which answers a question of Pandharipande; on the other hand, we prove that the cohomology ring admits a degeneration via the perverse filtration which is independent of the (nondegenerate) stability condition. The latter defines the intrinsic cohomology ring of the universal compactified Jacobian which only relies on g , n g,n . Our main tools include the support theorems, the recently developed Fourier theory for dualizable abelian fibrations, and the universal double ramification cycle relations associated with the universal Picard stack.



0.
Introduction

0.1.
Overview

Throughout, we work over the complex numbers
{\mathbb{C}}
.

The Jacobian variety associated with a nonsingular projective curve is a fundamental geometric object. Universally, we can consider the relative Jacobian over the moduli space
{\mathcal{M}}_{g}
of nonsingular genus
g
curves. The purpose of this paper is to systematically study the cohomology of the universal Jacobian over
{\mathcal{M}}_{g}
and its extensions over the moduli space
\overline{{\mathcal{M}}}_{g,n}
of stable marked curves.

Two subtle points arise along this path.

First, for different choices of the degree of the line bundles, the corresponding universal Jacobians are generally not isomorphic (nor birational); see
[
11
, Theorem 1.7]
. The degree
0
universal Jacobian is naturally an abelian scheme, and the degree
g-1
universal Jacobian admits a natural geometric theta divisor via the Abel–Jacobi map. Hence, there is no
a priori
reason to prefer one degree over another. Our first result (Theorem
0.1
) resolves this by showing that the
cohomology rings
of the universal Jacobians, for any degree, are isomorphic. This reduces the cohomological study of the Jacobian of any degree to the degree
0
case.

Second, when working over the moduli space of stable curves, the universal Jacobian admits compactifications known as universal
fine compactified Jacobians
; the geometry of a fine compactified Jacobian depends on the choice of a stability condition. As we will show in Theorem
0.6
, the cohomology ring of a fine compactified Jacobian also depends on the chosen stability condition. We will demonstrate in Theorem
0.7
that, although these cohomology rings may differ, they admit a common degeneration. This degeneration is constructed using the
perverse filtration
, and should be viewed as “the intrinsic cohomology ring” of the compactified Jacobian over the moduli space of stable curves, which does not rely on the stability condition.

We note that the perverse filtration has played a key role in the study of degenerations of Jacobians of curves. For example, the perverse filtration associated with the Hitchin system detects the weight filtration of the character variety via the (now proven)
P=W
conjecture in non-abelian Hodge theory
[
13
,
34
,
25
,
35
]
; the perverse filtration of a compactified Jacobian calculates the Gopakumar–Vafa invariants associated with a (possibly singular) curve
[
37
]
, and is closely related to knot invariants
[
48
,
38
,
43
]
. In this paper, the natural appearance of the perverse filtration is due to its compatibility with the Fourier transform developed in
[
35
]
.

0.2.
Jacobians

Let
{\mathcal{M}}_{g}
be the moduli space of nonsingular projective irreducible curves of genus
g
. The degree
d
universal Jacobian
J^{d}_{g}
parameterizes pairs

(C_{b},L),\quad[C_{b}]\in{\mathcal{M}}_{g},\quad L\in\mathrm{Pic}^{d}(C_{b});

it admits a natural morphism

\pi_{d}:J^{d}_{g}\to{\mathcal{M}}_{g},\quad(C_{b},L)\mapsto[C_{b}]

which is smooth and proper. As we mentioned earlier, the geometry of
J^{d}_{g}
is sensitive to
d
.

We consider the singular cohomology
H^{*}(J^{d}_{g},{\mathbb{Q}})
, which is naturally a graded algebra over
H^{*}({\mathcal{M}}_{g},{\mathbb{Q}})
via pullback and cup-product. Our first result shows that the cohomology of
J^{d}_{g}
is independent of
d
.

Theorem 0.1
.

For integers
d,d^{\prime}
, there is an isomorphism

H^{*}(J^{d}_{g},{\mathbb{Q}})\simeq H^{*}(J_{g}^{d^{\prime}},{\mathbb{Q}})

of graded
H^{*}({\mathcal{M}}_{g},{\mathbb{Q}})
-algebras.

We refer to Theorem
1.7
and Corollary
1.8
for a precise stronger statement. In fact, the cohomology
H^{*}(J^{d}_{g},{\mathbb{Q}})
is naturally bigraded with the second grading given by a splitting of the Leray filtration associated with
\pi_{d}:J^{d}_{g}\to{\mathcal{M}}_{g}
; the isomorphism in Theorem
0.1
is bigraded.

Remark 0.2
.

More generally, if we consider the universal Jacobian over the moduli space
{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}
of curves with
n
markings, the universal curve over
{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}
then admits a section if
n\geq 1
. In particular, in this case all the universal Jacobians of different degrees are identified via the section. Therefore Theorem
0.1
is nontrivial only when
n=0
.

0.3.
Compactified Jacobians

We consider a partial compactification of
{\mathcal{M}}_{g}
,

{\mathcal{M}}_{g}\subset\overline{{\mathcal{M}}}_{g}^{\mathrm{int}}\subset\overline{{\mathcal{M}}}_{g},

where
\overline{{\mathcal{M}}}_{g}^{\mathrm{int}}
is the moduli space of
integral
stable curves. For any degree
d
, the universal Jacobian
\pi_{d}:J^{d}_{g}\to{\mathcal{M}}_{g}
admits a natural extension

J^{d}_{g}\subset\overline{J}^{\mathrm{int},d}_{g}.

Here
\overline{J}^{\mathrm{int},d}_{g}
is the degree
d
universal
compactified Jacobian
parameterizing pairs
(C_{b},F)
with
[C_{b}]\in\overline{{\mathcal{M}}}^{\mathrm{int}}_{g}
an integral stable curve and
F
a (generically) rank
1
torsion-free sheaf on
C_{b}
satisfying

\chi(F)=d+1-g.

The universal compactified Jacobian admits a natural proper morphism

\pi_{d}:\overline{J}^{\mathrm{int},d}_{g}\to\overline{{\mathcal{M}}}_{g}^{\mathrm{int}},\quad(C_{b},F)\mapsto[C_{b}]

extending the smooth morphism
\pi_{d}:J^{d}_{g}\to{\mathcal{M}}_{g}
. For a singular nodal curve

[C_{b}]\in\overline{{\mathcal{M}}}_{g}^{\mathrm{int}}\setminus{\mathcal{M}}_{g},

the fiber
\pi_{d}^{-1}([C_{b}])
is the compactified Jacobian associated with the curve
C_{b}
, which is irreducible and contains the Jacobian of
C_{b}
(parameterizing line bundles) as a Zariski dense open subset.

The map
\pi_{d}:\overline{J}^{\mathrm{int},d}_{g}\to\overline{{\mathcal{M}}}_{g}^{\mathrm{int}}
endows the cohomology
H^{*}(\overline{J}^{\mathrm{int},d}_{g},{\mathbb{Q}})
with an extra structure — the perverse filtration
[
10
]
:

(1)

P_{0}H^{*}(\overline{J}^{\mathrm{int},d}_{g},{\mathbb{Q}})\subset P_{1}H^{*}(\overline{J}^{\mathrm{int},d}_{g},{\mathbb{Q}})\subset\cdots\subset P_{2g}H^{*}(\overline{J}^{\mathrm{int},d}_{g},{\mathbb{Q}})=H^{*}(\overline{J}_{g}^{\mathrm{int},d},{\mathbb{Q}}).

The following theorem was proven in
[
35
]
, which shows that the degeneration of the cohomology
H^{*}(\overline{J}^{\mathrm{int},d}_{g},{\mathbb{Q}})
via the perverse filtration is a bigraded
H^{*}(\overline{{\mathcal{M}}}_{g}^{\mathrm{int}},{\mathbb{Q}})
-algebra.

Theorem 0.3

(
[
35
]
)
.

The perverse filtration (
1
) is multiplicative with respect to the cup-product,
i.e.
, for integers
k,l
we have

\cup:P_{k}H^{*}(\overline{J}^{\mathrm{int},d}_{g},{\mathbb{Q}})\otimes P_{l}H^{*}(\overline{J}^{\mathrm{int},d}_{g},{\mathbb{Q}})\to P_{k+l}H^{*}(\overline{J}^{\mathrm{int},d}_{g},{\mathbb{Q}}).

In particular, the associated graded

{\mathbb{H}}^{\mathrm{int},d}_{g}:=\bigoplus_{k,m}\mathrm{Gr}^{P}_{k}H^{m}(\overline{J}^{\mathrm{int},d}_{g},{\mathbb{Q}})

is a bigraded
H^{*}(\overline{{\mathcal{M}}}^{\mathrm{int}}_{g},{\mathbb{Q}})
-algebra induced by the cup-product.

By definition, any element in
H^{m}(\overline{{\mathcal{M}}}^{\mathrm{int}}_{g},{\mathbb{Q}})
has bigrading
(0,m)
. Clearly,
{\mathbb{H}}^{\mathrm{int},d}_{g}
is isomorphic to
H^{*}(\overline{J}^{\mathrm{int},d}_{g},{\mathbb{Q}})
as
{\mathbb{Q}}
-vector spaces, but the ring structures may be different. The following result shows the
d
-independence of the algebra
{\mathbb{H}}^{\mathrm{int},d}_{g}
.

Theorem 0.4
.

For integers
d,d^{\prime}
, there is an isomorphism

{\mathbb{H}}^{\mathrm{int},d}_{g}\simeq{\mathbb{H}}^{\mathrm{int},d^{\prime}}_{g}

of bigraded
H^{*}(\overline{{\mathcal{M}}}^{\mathrm{int}}_{g},{\mathbb{Q}})
-algebras.

We in fact prove a stronger statement concerning a family of integral locally planar curves; see Theorem
2.6
and Corollary
2.7
. This recovers Theorem
0.4
immediately since nodal singularities are planar.

We can also consider the universal compactified Jacobian over the locus of integral curves in
\overline{{\mathcal{M}}}_{g,n}
; but as in Remark
0.2
, there are obvious isomorphisms between the compactified Jacobians of various degrees as long as
n\geq 1
.

Remark 0.5
.

It is natural to ask if there is an isomorphism

H^{*}(\overline{J}^{\mathrm{int},d}_{g},{\mathbb{Q}})\simeq H^{*}(\overline{J}^{\mathrm{int},d^{\prime}}_{g},{\mathbb{Q}})

of graded
H^{*}(\overline{{\mathcal{M}}}^{\mathrm{int}}_{g},{\mathbb{Q}})
-algebras without passing to the associated graded. In view of Theorem
0.6
below, we expect that the answer is negative.

0.4.
Fine compactified Jacobians

Next, we consider the moduli space
\overline{{\mathcal{M}}}_{g,n}
of stable curves with
n
markings. In order to further extend the compactified Jacobians to the locus of reducible curves,
stability conditions
are needed. In this paper we work with the stability conditions introduced by Kass–Pagani
[
27
]
, and independently by Melo
[
39
]
.

Roughly, a stability condition in the sense of
[
27
]
is an assignment of a rational number to every irreducible component of every stable marked curve

(C_{b},x_{1},\cdots,x_{n})\in\overline{{\mathcal{M}}}_{g,n},

satisfying certain compatibility conditions.
When we fix the degree
d
, the space of stability conditions has a wall-and-chamber structure. The universal
fine compactified Jacobian
for a given stability condition is the moduli space parameterizing rank
1
torsion-free sheaves on stable marked curves which satisfy the stability inequality. We denote it by
\overline{J}_{g,n}^{d,\phi}
to indicate its dependence on the genus
g
, the number of markings
n
, the degree of the torsion-free sheaves
d
, and the stability condition
\phi
. For a
nondegenerate
stability condition
\phi
,
i.e.
, there are no strictly semistable sheaves, the universal fine compactified Jacobian
\overline{J}_{g,n}^{d,\phi}
is a nonsingular proper Deligne–Mumford stack which contains the universal compactified Jacobian of integral curves as a Zariski dense open subset.

The following shows that the cohomology ring of
\overline{J}_{g,n}^{d,\phi}
depends on the stability condition
\phi
; this answers a question of Pandharipande
[
51
, Question A]
.

Theorem 0.6
.

For
g\geq 4
and any integers
d,d^{\prime}
, there exist nondegenerate stability conditions
\phi,\phi^{\prime}
of degrees
d,d^{\prime}
respectively such that

H^{*}(\overline{J}_{g,1}^{d,\phi},{\mathbb{Q}})\not\simeq H^{*}(\overline{J}_{g,1}^{d^{\prime},\phi^{\prime}},{\mathbb{Q}})

as graded
H^{*}(\overline{{\mathcal{M}}}_{g,1},{\mathbb{Q}})
-algebras.

On the other hand, we may consider the perverse filtration
P_{\bullet}H^{*}(\overline{J}_{g,n}^{d,\phi},{\mathbb{Q}})
associated with the natural proper morphism

\pi_{d}:\overline{J}_{g,n}^{d,\phi}\to\overline{{\mathcal{M}}}_{g,n}

and the associated graded

{\mathbb{H}}^{d,\phi}_{g,n}:=\bigoplus_{k,m}\mathrm{Gr}_{k}^{P}H^{m}(\overline{J}^{d,\phi}_{g,n},{\mathbb{Q}}).

Theorem 0.7
.

Assume that
\phi,\phi^{\prime}
are nondegenerate stability conditions of degrees
d,d^{\prime}
respectively, and that
n\geq 1
. We have the following.

(i)

The associated graded
{\mathbb{H}}^{d,\phi}_{g,n}
is a bigraded algebra over
H^{*}(\overline{{\mathcal{M}}}_{g,n},{\mathbb{Q}})
with elements in
H^{m}(\overline{{\mathcal{M}}}_{g,n},{\mathbb{Q}})
of bigrading
(0,m)
.

(ii)

There is an isomorphism

{\mathbb{H}}^{d,\phi}_{g,n}\simeq{\mathbb{H}}^{d^{\prime},\phi^{\prime}}_{g,n}

of bigraded
H^{*}(\overline{{\mathcal{M}}}_{g,n},{\mathbb{Q}})
-algebras.

Theorems
0.6
and
0.7
suggest that the induced cup-product on the associated graded with respect to the perverse filtration is “intrinsic” to the moduli space of stable marked curves.

Remark 0.8
.

When
n=0
, nondegenerate stability conditions of degree
d
exist if and only if

(2)

\mathrm{gcd}(d+1-g,2g-2)=1;

see
[
27
, Remark 5.12]
. Thus, the best we can expect is that both statements of Theorem
0.7
hold for those values of
d
satisfying the numerical condition (
2
). Our proof does not cover this case due to the absence of a nondegenerate stability condition of degree
0
.

We note that the proof of Theorem
0.7
also yields a parallel result for fine compactified Jacobians of a reduced locally planar curve which may be of independent interest. To state the result, now we assume that
C_{0}
is a reduced locally planar curve of arithmetic genus
g
. For any choice of a nondegenerate stability condition
\phi
,
i.e.
, there are no strictly semistable sheaves, Migliorini–Shende–Viviani
[
44
]
introduced a perverse filtration on the cohomology of the corresponding fine compactified Jacobian

(3)

P_{0}H^{*}(\overline{J}^{\phi}_{C_{0}},{\mathbb{Q}})\subset P_{1}H^{*}(\overline{J}^{\phi}_{C_{0}},{\mathbb{Q}})\subset\cdots\subset P_{2g}H^{*}(\overline{J}^{\phi}_{C_{0}},{\mathbb{Q}})=H^{*}(\overline{J}^{\phi}_{C_{0}},{\mathbb{Q}}).

Similarly, we consider the associated graded

{\mathbb{H}}^{\phi}_{C_{0}}:=\bigoplus_{k,m}\mathrm{Gr}_{k}^{P}H^{m}(\overline{J}_{C_{0}}^{\phi},{\mathbb{Q}}).

Theorem 0.9
.

Assume that
\phi,\phi^{\prime}
are nondegenerate stability conditions for the reduced locally planar curve
C_{0}
. We have the following.

(i)

The associated graded
{\mathbb{H}}^{\phi}_{C_{0}}
is a bigraded
{\mathbb{Q}}
-algebra.

(ii)

There is an isomorphism

{\mathbb{H}}^{\phi}_{C_{0}}\simeq{\mathbb{H}}^{\phi^{\prime}}_{C_{0}}

of bigraded
{\mathbb{Q}}
-algebras.

Theorems
0.7
and
0.9
are deduced from a more general sheaf-theoretic statement for relative fine compactified Jacobians associated with locally planar reduced curves; see Theorem
3.13
.

0.5.
Ideas of the proofs

The key idea in the proofs of Theorems
0.1
,
0.4
,
0.7
, and
0.9
is the observation that the Fourier transform controls the cup product. For example, to show the
d
-independence
of the cohomology ring of the universal Jacobians
J^{d}_{g}
over
{\mathcal{M}}_{g}
, we establish a Fourier transform connecting
J^{d}_{g}
with
J^{0}_{g}
, which allows us to relate the cup-product for
J^{d}_{g}
to the convolution product for
J^{0}_{g}
:

(4)

(H^{*}(J^{d}_{g},{\mathbb{Q}}),\cup)\longleftarrow\mathrm{Fourier~Transform}\longrightarrow(H^{*}(J^{0}_{g},{\mathbb{Q}}),\ast^{d}).

Then the
d
-independence of the cup product is reduced to the
d
-independence of the convolution product, which is more manageable. This proves Theorem
0.1
.

To carry out this idea when there are singular curves, we apply the Fourier transform induced by the sheaves constructed by Arinkin
[
3
]
and Melo–Rapagnetta–Viviani
[
41
]
for the Fourier–Mukai duality. By the recent work of Maulik–Shen–Yin
[
35
]
, the Fourier transform interacts naturally with the (multiplicative) perverse filtration. Furthermore, we show in this paper that the Fourier transform and the induced cup product on the associated graded (with respect to the perverse filtration) provides an analogue of (
4
) when there are singular curves. For example, in the setting of Theorem
0.7
we have

(5)

\left(\mathrm{Gr}^{P}_{\bullet}H^{*}(\overline{J}^{d,\phi}_{g,n},{\mathbb{Q}}),\overline{\cup}\right)\longleftarrow\mathrm{Fourier~Transform}\longrightarrow\left(\mathrm{Gr}^{P}_{\bullet}H^{*}(\overline{J}^{0,\phi_{0}}_{g,n},{\mathbb{Q}}),\overline{\ast}_{\mathrm{red}}^{\phi}\right)

where
\phi_{0}
is a fixed nondegenerate stability condition and
\overline{\ast}_{\mathrm{red}}^{\phi}
is the reduced convolution product — the natural convolution product on the associated graded. Again, we complete the proof of Theorem
0.7
by showing that the reduced convolution product on the right-hand side of (
5
), which
a priori
relies on
\phi
, is in fact independent of
d,\phi
.

From the perspective of the Fourier transform, the induced cup product on the associated graded with respect to the perverse filtration is a more natural ring structure on the cohomology group of the universal compactified Jacobian. We view this as the
intrinsic cohomology ring
of the universal compactified Jacobian; this ring is defined via a choice of compactification, but is eventually independent of the compactification. It is interesting to explore if the intrinsic cohomology ring can be realized
geometrically
,
i.e.
, as (part of) the cohomology ring of some space related to the universal Jacobian.

On the other hand, we prove in Theorem
0.6
that the actual ring structure of
H^{*}(\overline{J}^{d,\phi}_{g,n},{\mathbb{Q}})
is very sensitive to the choice of nondegenerate stability condition
\phi
; this can already be seen by considering monomials of divisors on fine compactified Jacobians.

0.6.
Relations to other work and further discussions

We conclude the introduction by discussing some relations to other work.

0.6.1.
Compactified Jacobians

There are other versions of (relative) compactified Jacobians, which generalize the ones given by the polarization stability condition considered in this paper; we refer to
[
49
,
23
]
and the references therein for more details. We expect that our theory via the Fourier–Mukai duality and the support theorem can also be applied to these versions.

0.6.2.
Enhanced
\chi
-independence

The relative Jacobian (of a given degree) of the moduli space of nonsingular degree
d
planar curves admits a natural compactification by Le Potier
[
31
]
. The Le Potier moduli space
M_{d,\chi}
parameterizes semistable
1
-dimensional sheaves on
{\mathbb{P}}^{2}
with Fitting support of degree
d
and Euler characteristic
\chi
. We assume
(d,\chi)=1
so that the moduli spaces are nonsingular; a story parallel to the case of stable curves is expected to hold for this compactification:

(i)

The cohomology group
H^{*}(M_{d,\chi},{\mathbb{Q}})
is known to be
\chi
-independent
[
12
,
33
]
.

(ii)

The ring structure of
H^{*}(M_{d,\chi},{\mathbb{Q}})
is highly sensitive to
\chi

[
32
]
.

(iii)

It was conjectured by Kononov–Moreira–Lim–Pi that the associated graded

\mathrm{Gr}^{P}_{\bullet}H^{*}(M_{d,\chi},{\mathbb{Q}})

with respect to the perverse filtration (defined by the support map
M_{d,\chi}\to|{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathbb{P}}^{2}}(d)|
) is naturally a
{\mathbb{Q}}
-algebra induced by the cup-product, which is further
\chi
-independent; this was confirmed for
d=5

[
28
]
.

Since the Le Potier compactification of the relative Jacobian of planar curves contains fibers which are not given by fine compactified Jacobians of reduced curves, our theory (which relies on the Fourier–Mukai duality) cannot be applied to prove the conjecture (iii). On the other hand, the recently discovered
P=C
phenomenon for
M_{d,\chi}

[
29
,
28
]
suggests that a version of the Arinkin sheaf may be extended over the non-reduced planar curves.

0.6.3.
Lagrangian fibrations

In general, the induced cup product — assuming it is well defined — on the associated graded with respect to the perverse filtration does not yield the same ring as the ordinary cohomology ring (
e.g.
one can compare Theorem
0.6
with Theorem
0.7
). On the other hand, it was conjectured (
c.f.

[
5
, Section 3]
) that the perverse filtration associated with a
Lagrangian fibration

f:M\to B
admits a multiplicative splitting (even
motivically
); in particular, one expects an isomorphism

(6)

\left(\mathrm{Gr}^{P}_{\bullet}H^{*}(M,{\mathbb{Q}}),\overline{\cup}\right)\simeq\left(H^{*}(M,{\mathbb{Q}}),\cup\right).

For Lagrangian fibrations associated with compact hyper-Kähler varieties, this was proven in
[
53
]
using the Hodge decomposition and the Looijenga–Lunts–Verbitsky Lie algebra; for the Hitchin system associated with
\mathrm{GL}_{n}
, this is a consequence of the
P=W
conjecture
[
13
,
34
,
25
,
35
]
.

The isomorphism (
6
) is further expected to hold sheaf-theoretically over
B
, and the discussion above suggests that its obstruction is global. Hence, we expect that the “local” perverse filtration (
3
) admits a multiplicative splitting. This, combined with Theorem
0.9
, leads us to conjecture the following.

Conjecture 0.10
.

Under the assumption of Theorem
0.9
, for the nondegenerate stability conditions
\phi,\phi^{\prime}
, there is an isomorphism

H^{*}(\overline{J}^{\phi}_{C_{0}},{\mathbb{Q}})\simeq H^{*}(\overline{J}^{\phi^{\prime}}_{C_{0}},{\mathbb{Q}})

of graded
{\mathbb{Q}}
-algebras.

0.6.4.
Intrinsic tautological ring

For fine compactified Jacobians
\overline{J}_{g,n}^{d,\phi}
over the moduli space of stable curves, the universal family induces a natural notion of tautological subring

RH^{*}(\overline{J}_{g,n}^{d,\phi})\subset H^{*}(\overline{J}_{g,n}^{d,\phi},{\mathbb{Q}});

we refer to Section
4.2
and the references therein for details.

In analogy with the intrinsic cohomology ring, it is natural to take the associated graded of the tautological ring with respect to the perverse filtration

(7)

\mathbb{RH}^{d,\phi}_{g,n}:=\bigoplus_{k,m}\mathrm{Gr}_{k}^{P}RH^{m}(\overline{J}^{d,\phi}_{g,n})\subset\mathbb{H}^{d,\phi}_{g,n}.

Question 0.11
.

Does the isomorphism in Theorem
0.7
(ii) respect the subring (
7
)? If so, can we describe the structure of
\mathbb{RH}^{d,\phi}_{g,n}
?

0.7.
Acknowledgements

We are grateful to Dima Arinkin, Soumik Ghosh, Sam Molcho, Miguel Moreira, Rahul Pandharipande, Weite Pi, Aaron Pixton, and Johannes Schmitt for helpful discussions. The results proven here were first presented by J.S. and Q.Y. in the workshop “A Saturday of Moduli” at ETH Zürich in June 2025.

Y.B. was supported by June E Huh Visiting Fellowship. D.M. was supported by a Simons Investigator Grant. J.S. was supported by the NSF Grant DMS-2301474 and a Sloan Research Fellowship.

1.
Jacobians

This section focuses on nonsingular curves and their Jacobians. We consider Jacobians of all degrees associated with a family of nonsingular curves, and show that their cohomology rings are all isomorphic and hence independent of the degree; see Theorem
1.7
and Corollary
1.8
for precise statements. The proof closely follows the arguments of Beauville
[
8
,
9
]
.

1.1.
Fourier transforms

Let
C\to B
be a smooth projective family of irreducible curves of genus
g
over a nonsingular irreducible quasiprojective base variety
B
. Later,
B
can also be the moduli stack of nonsingular curves
{\mathcal{M}}_{g}
; see Remark
1.9
. For any integer
d
, let

\pi_{d}:J^{d}_{C}\to B

be the associated Jacobian family of degree
d
line bundles. Note that by
[
42
]
, in general
J^{d}_{C}
is a fine moduli space if and only if

\mathrm{gcd}(d+1-g,2g-2)=1.

To overcome this issue and to make use of the Poincaré line bundle, we follow the strategy of
[
35
]
with simplifications in the nonsingular case; see Remark
1.1
.

We choose a multisection

(8)

D\subset C\to B

finite and flat of degree
r
over
B
, whose existence is guaranteed by the quasiprojectivity of
B
and by applying
[
14
, Theorem 1.1]
. By Bertini we may assume
D
to be nonsingular. For any
B
-scheme
T
, a flat family of degree
d
line bundles

{\mathcal{F}}^{d}_{T}\rightsquigarrow C\times_{B}T

over
T
is said to be
trivialized along the multisection
D
if there is a specified isomorphism

\mathrm{Norm}({\mathcal{F}}^{d}_{T}|_{D\times_{B}T})\simeq{\mathcal{O}}_{T}.

Here
\mathrm{Norm}:\mathop{\rm Pic}\nolimits(D\times_{B}T)\to\mathop{\rm Pic}\nolimits(T)
is the norm map with respect to the finite and flat morphism
D\times_{B}T\to T
; see
[
55
, Tag 0BD2]
. Then, by considering families of line bundles trivialized along
D
, we obtain a
\mu_{r}
-gerbe

\mathcal{J}^{d}_{C}\to J_{C}^{d}

equipped with a universal line bundle

(9)

{\mathcal{F}}^{d}\in\mathrm{Coh}(C\times_{B}{\mathcal{J}}^{d}_{C})_{(1)},

where
\mathrm{Coh}(C\times_{B}{\mathcal{J}}^{d}_{C})_{(1)}
is the isotypic category consisting of coherent sheaves on which the action of
\mu_{r}
is given by the character
\lambda\mapsto\lambda^{1}
; see
[
35
, Section 4.2]
for more details.

Remark 1.1
.

A subtle difference between the trivialization process here and in
[
35
, Section 4.2]
is the norm map versus pushing forward and taking the determinant. The norm map, being a group homomorphism, has better functorial properties and results in a better-behaved universal line bundle (
9
). On the other hand, when dealing with rank
1
torsion-free sheaves we do not have a norm map at hand, hence the treatment in
[
35
, Section 4.2]
.

Throughout this section we set

{\mathcal{J}}_{C}^{\vee}:={\mathcal{J}}_{C}^{0},\quad J_{C}^{\vee}:=J_{C}^{0},\quad\pi^{\vee}:=\pi_{0}:J_{C}^{\vee}\to B

and let
{\mathcal{J}}_{C}^{\vee}
serve as the dual to
\mathcal{J}^{d}_{C}
for all
d\in{\mathbb{Z}}
at once. By
[
35
, Proposition 4.3]
, there exists a Poincaré line bundle

{\mathcal{P}}^{d}\in\mathrm{Coh}({\mathcal{J}}_{C}^{\vee}\times_{B}{\mathcal{J}}^{d}_{C})_{(d,0)}

where the subscript
(d,0)
specifies the character
\lambda\mapsto\lambda^{d}
(resp.
\lambda\mapsto\lambda^{0}
) of the
\mu_{r}
-action on the
{\mathcal{J}}^{\vee}_{C}
(resp.
{\mathcal{J}}_{C}^{d}
) factor. The Poincaré line bundle induces a Fourier–Mukai transform

\mathrm{FM}_{{\mathcal{P}}^{d}}:D^{b}\mathrm{Coh}({\mathcal{J}}_{C}^{\vee})_{(-d)}\xrightarrow{\simeq}D^{b}\mathrm{Coh}({\mathcal{J}}^{d}_{C})_{(0)}

between the corresponding isotypic bounded derived categories. Its inverse is given by the Fourier–Mukai kernel

({\mathcal{P}}^{d})^{-1}:=({\mathcal{P}}^{d})^{\vee}\otimes p_{{\mathcal{J}}^{d}_{C}}^{*}\omega_{{\mathcal{J}}^{d}_{C}/B}[g]\in D^{b}\mathrm{Coh}({\mathcal{J}}^{d}_{C}\times_{B}{\mathcal{J}}^{\vee}_{C})_{(0,-d)}

where
p_{{\mathcal{J}}^{d}_{C}}:{\mathcal{J}}^{d}_{C}\times_{B}{\mathcal{J}}^{\vee}_{C}\to{\mathcal{J}}^{d}_{C}
is the projection and
\omega_{{\mathcal{J}}^{d}_{C}/B}
is the relative canonical bundle of
{\mathcal{J}}^{d}_{C}\to B
.

The quasiprojectivity of
B
also ensures that the Deligne–Mumford stack
{\mathcal{J}}^{d}_{C}
is a quotient stack. We can then make use of the Chow theory of quotient stacks
[
18
,
19
]
to define the Chow-theoretic Fourier transform. More precisely, we set

(10)

{\mathfrak{F}}^{d}=\sum_{i}{\mathfrak{F}}^{d}_{i}:={\mathrm{ch}}({\mathcal{P}}^{d})\in\mathrm{CH}^{*}(J_{C}^{\vee}\times_{B}J^{d}_{C}),\quad{\mathfrak{F}}^{d}_{i}:={\mathrm{ch}}_{i}({\mathcal{P}}^{d})\in\mathrm{CH}^{i}(J_{C}^{\vee}\times_{B}J^{d}_{C})

where all Chow groups and rings are taken with
{\mathbb{Q}}
-coefficients, and the Chow groups of gerbes are identified with those of the coarse spaces. We view
{\mathfrak{F}}^{d}
as a mixed-degree Chow correspondence between
J_{C}^{\vee}
and
J^{d}_{C}
, whose inverse is defined accordingly via Riemann–Roch:

({\mathfrak{F}}^{d})^{-1}=\sum_{i}({\mathfrak{F}}^{d})^{-1}_{i}\in\mathrm{CH}^{*}(J_{C}^{d}\times_{B}J^{\vee}_{C}),\quad({\mathfrak{F}}^{d})^{-1}_{i}\in\mathrm{CH}^{i}(J_{C}^{d}\times_{B}J^{\vee}_{C}).

We have

(11)

[\Delta_{J^{d}_{C}/B}]={\mathfrak{F}}^{d}\circ({\mathfrak{F}}^{d})^{-1}\in\mathrm{CH}^{g}(J_{C}^{d}\times_{B}J^{d}_{C}),\quad[\Delta_{J^{\vee}_{C}/B}]=({\mathfrak{F}}^{d})^{-1}\circ{\mathfrak{F}}^{d}\in\mathrm{CH}^{g}(J_{C}^{\vee}\times_{B}J^{\vee}_{C})

where
[\Delta_{J^{d}_{C}/B}]
(resp.
[\Delta_{J^{\vee}_{C}/B}]
) is the relative diagonal class of
\pi_{d}
(resp.
\pi^{\vee}
), and serves as the identity element in the ring of Chow self-correspondences.

Two main ingredients in establishing Theorem
1.7
are the Fourier vanishing and the convolution product, which will be addressed in the next two sections.

1.2.
Fourier vanishing

For the smooth family
\pi_{d}:J^{d}_{C}\to B
, the
full
Fourier vanishing (FV) refers to the following statement.

Proposition 1.2

(Fourier vanishing)
.

For all
i+j\neq 2g
, we have

({\mathfrak{F}}^{d})^{-1}_{j}\circ{\mathfrak{F}}^{d}_{i}=0\in\mathrm{CH}^{i+j-g}(J_{C}^{\vee}\times_{B}J_{C}^{\vee}).

We first develop some consequences assuming (FV). We define, for
0\leq i\leq 2g
, Chow self-correspondences

(12)

{\mathfrak{p}}^{d}_{i}:={\mathfrak{F}}^{d}_{i}\circ({\mathfrak{F}}^{d})^{-1}_{2g-i}\in\mathrm{CH}^{g}(J_{C}^{d}\times_{B}J^{d}_{C}),\quad{\mathfrak{p}}^{\vee}_{i}:=({\mathfrak{F}}^{d})^{-1}_{i}\circ{\mathfrak{F}}^{d}_{2g-i}\in\mathrm{CH}^{g}(J^{\vee}_{C}\times_{B}J^{\vee}_{C}).

For dimension reasons, the relative diagonal class
[\Delta_{J^{d}_{C}/B}]
(resp.
[\Delta_{J^{\vee}_{C}/B}]
) decomposes as

[\Delta_{J^{d}_{C}/B}]={\mathfrak{F}}^{d}\circ({\mathfrak{F}}^{d})^{-1}=\sum_{i=0}^{2g}{\mathfrak{p}}_{i}^{d},\quad[\Delta_{J^{\vee}_{C}/B}]=({\mathfrak{F}}^{d})^{-1}\circ{\mathfrak{F}}^{d}=\sum_{i=0}^{2g}{\mathfrak{p}}_{i}^{\vee}.

Applying (FV), we find immediately the relations

{\mathfrak{p}}_{i}^{d}\circ{\mathfrak{p}}_{j}^{d}=\begin{cases}{\mathfrak{p}}_{i}^{d}&i=j\\
0&i\neq j;\end{cases}

in other words, the Chow self-correspondences
{\mathfrak{p}}^{d}_{i}
are pairwise orthogonal idempotents. We also have the corresponding statement for the
{\mathfrak{p}}^{\vee}_{i}
which furthermore justifies the notation.

Proposition 1.3
.

For
0\leq i\leq 2g
, the Chow self-correspondences
{\mathfrak{p}}^{\vee}_{i}
in (
12
) are pairwise orthogonal idempotents which are independent of the degree
d
.

The proof of Proposition
1.3
will be given at the end of this section, after the proof of (FV).

As our applications are mostly cohomological, we shall state some results in terms of constructible sheaves. We consider the derived pushforward of the constant sheaves

\pi_{d*}{\mathbb{Q}}_{J_{C}^{d}},\,\pi^{\vee}_{*}{\mathbb{Q}}_{J_{C}^{\vee}}\in D^{b}_{c}(B)

in the bounded constructible category of
B
. Here and throughout, all pushforward, pullback, and tensor functors are derived. The Fourier transform (
10
) and its inverse induce morphisms of the (shifted) pushforward complexes via the cycle class maps

\displaystyle\mathrm{CH}^{i}(J_{C}^{\vee}\times_{B}J^{d}_{C})\to H^{2i}(J_{C}^{\vee}\times_{B}J^{d}_{C},{\mathbb{Q}})\simeq\operatorname{Hom}_{D^{b}_{c}(B)}(\pi^{\vee}_{*}{\mathbb{Q}}_{J_{C}^{\vee}},\pi_{d*}{\mathbb{Q}}_{J_{C}^{d}}[2i-2g]),

\displaystyle\mathrm{CH}^{i}(J_{C}^{d}\times_{B}J_{C}^{\vee})\to H^{2i}(J_{C}^{d}\times_{B}J^{\vee}_{C},{\mathbb{Q}})\simeq\operatorname{Hom}_{D^{b}_{c}(B)}(\pi_{d*}{\mathbb{Q}}_{J_{C}^{d}},\pi^{\vee}_{*}{\mathbb{Q}}_{J_{C}^{\vee}}[2i-2g]);

see
[
35
, Section 2.2.2]
. We thus obtain the sheaf-theoretic Fourier transform

(13)

{\mathfrak{F}}^{d}=\sum_{i}{\mathfrak{F}}^{d}_{i}:\pi^{\vee}_{*}{\mathbb{Q}}_{J_{C}^{\vee}}\to\pi_{d*}{\mathbb{Q}}_{J_{C}^{d}}[-],\quad{\mathfrak{F}}^{d}_{i}:\pi^{\vee}_{*}{\mathbb{Q}}_{J_{C}^{\vee}}\to\pi_{d*}{\mathbb{Q}}_{J_{C}^{d}}[2i-2g]

and its inverse

({\mathfrak{F}}^{d})^{-1}=\sum_{i}({\mathfrak{F}}^{d})^{-1}_{i}:\pi_{d*}{\mathbb{Q}}_{J_{C}^{d}}\to\pi^{\vee}_{*}{\mathbb{Q}}_{J_{C}^{\vee}}[-],\quad({\mathfrak{F}}^{d})^{-1}_{i}:\pi_{d*}{\mathbb{Q}}_{J_{C}^{d}}\to\pi^{\vee}_{*}{\mathbb{Q}}_{J_{C}^{\vee}}[2i-2g].

The Chow self-correspondences in (
12
) then induce (unshifted) endomorphisms

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{p}}^{d}_{i}={\mathfrak{F}}^{d}_{i}\circ({\mathfrak{F}}^{d})^{-1}_{2g-i}:\pi_{d*}{\mathbb{Q}}_{J_{C}^{d}}\to\pi_{d*}{\mathbb{Q}}_{J_{C}^{d}},

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{p}}^{\vee}_{i}=({\mathfrak{F}}^{d})^{-1}_{i}\circ{\mathfrak{F}}^{d}_{2g-i}:\pi_{*}^{\vee}{\mathbb{Q}}_{J_{C}^{\vee}}\to\pi_{*}^{\vee}{\mathbb{Q}}_{J_{C}^{\vee}}.

By (FV) and Proposition
1.3
, the sheaf-theoretic
{\mathfrak{p}}_{i}^{d}
(resp.
{\mathfrak{p}}_{i}^{\vee}
) are pairwise orthogonal idempotents satisfying

(14)

\mathrm{id}_{\pi_{d*}{\mathbb{Q}}_{J_{C}^{d}}}={\mathfrak{F}}^{d}\circ({\mathfrak{F}}^{d})^{-1}=\sum_{i=0}^{2g}{\mathfrak{p}}_{i}^{d},\quad\mathrm{id}_{\pi^{\vee}_{*}{\mathbb{Q}}_{J_{C}^{\vee}}}=({\mathfrak{F}}^{d})^{-1}\circ{\mathfrak{F}}^{d}=\sum_{i=0}^{2g}{\mathfrak{p}}_{i}^{\vee}.

This, together with the fact that the category
D^{b}_{c}(B)
is pseudo-abelian
[
15
, Lemma 2.24]
, yields natural decompositions of the pushforward complexes
\pi_{d*}{\mathbb{Q}}_{J_{C}^{d}}
and
\pi_{*}^{\vee}{\mathbb{Q}}_{J_{C}^{\vee}}
.

Proposition 1.4

(
[
17
, Remark 2 after Corollary 3.2]
and
[
35
, Corollaries 4.6 and 4.7]
)
.

The identities (
14
) induce direct sum decompositions

\displaystyle\pi_{d*}{\mathbb{Q}}_{J_{C}^{d}}=\bigoplus_{i=0}^{2g}(\pi_{d*}{\mathbb{Q}}_{J_{C}^{d}})_{(i)},\quad(\pi_{d*}{\mathbb{Q}}_{J_{C}^{d}})_{(i)}:=\mathrm{Im}({\mathfrak{p}}_{i}^{d})\in D^{b}_{c}(B),

\displaystyle\pi_{*}^{\vee}{\mathbb{Q}}_{J_{C}^{\vee}}=\bigoplus_{i=0}^{2g}(\pi_{*}^{\vee}{\mathbb{Q}}_{J_{C}^{\vee}})_{(i)},\quad(\pi_{*}^{\vee}{\mathbb{Q}}_{J_{C}^{\vee}})_{(i)}:=\mathrm{Im}({\mathfrak{p}}_{i}^{\vee})\in D^{b}_{c}(B).

Moreover, for
0\leq i\leq 2g
we have

(\pi_{d*}{\mathbb{Q}}_{J_{C}^{d}})_{(i)}\simeq H^{i}(\pi_{d*}{\mathbb{Q}}_{J_{C}^{d}})[-i],\quad(\pi_{*}^{\vee}{\mathbb{Q}}_{J_{C}^{\vee}})_{(i)}\simeq H^{i}(\pi_{*}^{\vee}{\mathbb{Q}}_{J_{C}^{\vee}})[-i].

Note that the decomposition theorem
[
16
]
for smooth maps is in general
not
canonical; the content of Proposition
1.4
is that the Fourier transform provides a natural choice for both
\pi_{d}:J_{C}^{d}\to B
and
\pi^{\vee}:J^{\vee}_{C}\to B
. Taking global cohomology, we obtain direct sum decompositions

\displaystyle H^{*}(J_{C}^{d},{\mathbb{Q}})=\bigoplus_{i=0}^{2g}H^{*}(J^{d}_{C},{\mathbb{Q}})_{(i)},\quad H^{*}(J^{d}_{C},{\mathbb{Q}})_{(i)}:=\mathrm{Im}\left({\mathfrak{p}}^{d}_{i}:H^{*}(J^{d}_{C},{\mathbb{Q}})\to H^{*}(J^{d}_{C},{\mathbb{Q}})\right),

\displaystyle H^{*}(J_{C}^{\vee},{\mathbb{Q}})=\bigoplus_{i=0}^{2g}H^{*}(J^{\vee}_{C},{\mathbb{Q}})_{(i)},\quad H^{*}(J^{\vee}_{C},{\mathbb{Q}})_{(i)}:=\mathrm{Im}\left({\mathfrak{p}}^{\vee}_{i}:H^{*}(J^{\vee}_{C},{\mathbb{Q}})\to H^{*}(J^{\vee}_{C},{\mathbb{Q}})\right).

The proof of (FV), following Beauville, uses the “multiplication by
N
” map of the abelian scheme
J^{\vee}_{C}
:

(15)

[N]:J^{\vee}_{C}\to J^{\vee}_{C},\quad N\in{\mathbb{Z}}.

It is ultimately reduced to the following lemma.

Lemma 1.5
.

For
N\equiv 1\pmod{r}
, we have

([N]\times\mathrm{id}_{J^{d}_{C}})^{*}c_{1}({\mathcal{P}}^{d})=Nc_{1}({\mathcal{P}}^{d})\in\mathrm{CH}^{1}(J_{C}^{\vee}\times_{B}J_{C}^{d}).

Proof.

First, for
N\equiv 1\pmod{r}
, we consider the
N
-th tensor power of the universal sheaf

({\mathcal{F}}^{\vee})^{\otimes N}\in\mathrm{Coh}(C\times_{B}{\mathcal{J}}^{\vee}_{C})_{(1)}.

Since the norm map is a group homomorphism, we obtain a trivialization along the multisection
D
in (
8
):

\mathrm{Norm}\left(({\mathcal{F}}^{\vee})^{\otimes N}|_{D\times_{B}{\mathcal{J}}^{\vee}_{C}}\right)\simeq\left(\mathrm{Norm}({\mathcal{F}}^{\vee}|_{D\times_{B}{\mathcal{J}}^{\vee}_{C}})\right)^{\otimes N}\simeq{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{J}}^{\vee}_{C}}.

This yields a morphism

[N]:{\mathcal{J}}^{\vee}_{C}\to{\mathcal{J}}^{\vee}_{C}

lifting (
15
) and under which we have

(\mathrm{id}_{C}\times[N])^{*}{\mathcal{F}}^{\vee}\simeq({\mathcal{F}}^{\vee})^{\otimes N}\in\mathrm{Coh}(C\times_{B}{\mathcal{J}}^{\vee}_{C}).

Next, we compare the pullback of the Poincaré line bundle
([N]\times\mathrm{id}_{{\mathcal{J}}^{d}_{C}})^{*}{\mathcal{P}}^{d}
with
({\mathcal{P}}^{d})^{\otimes N}
, both living in
\mathrm{Coh}({\mathcal{J}}^{\vee}_{C}\times_{B}{\mathcal{J}}^{d}_{C})_{(d,0)}
for
N\equiv 1\pmod{r}
. In fact, for the purpose of the first Chern class (with
{\mathbb{Q}}
-coefficients) it suffices to perform a base change along
D\to B
and compare the pullback of the line bundles. In other words, up to replacing
B
by
D
, we may assume that
C\to B
also admits a section

(16)

s:B\to C.

The section yields another untwisted universal line bundle

{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}\in\mathrm{Coh}(C\times_{B}J^{\vee}_{C})

which is trivialized along both
s_{J^{\vee}_{C}}:J^{\vee}_{C}\to C\times_{B}J^{\vee}_{C}
and the zero section
0_{C}:C\to C\times_{B}J^{\vee}_{C}
.
After the natural identification

\mathrm{Coh}(C\times_{B}J^{\vee}_{C})\simeq\mathrm{Coh}(C\times_{B}{\mathcal{J}}^{\vee}_{C})_{(0)}

we have

{\mathcal{F}}^{\vee}\simeq{\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}\otimes p_{{\mathcal{J}}^{\vee}_{C}}^{*}{\mathcal{L}}\in\mathrm{Coh}(C\times_{B}{\mathcal{J}}^{\vee}_{C})_{(1)},

where
{\mathcal{L}}\in\mathrm{Coh}({\mathcal{J}}^{\vee}_{C})_{(1)}
is a line bundle and
p_{{\mathcal{J}}^{\vee}_{C}}:C\times_{B}{\mathcal{J}}^{\vee}_{C}\to{\mathcal{J}}^{\vee}_{C}
is the projection. We then switch back to the multisection
D
in (
8
) and find

\mathrm{Norm}({\mathcal{F}}^{\vee}|_{D\times_{B}{\mathcal{J}}^{\vee}_{C}})\simeq\mathrm{Norm}({\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}|_{D\times_{B}{\mathcal{J}}^{\vee}_{C}})\otimes{\mathcal{L}}^{\otimes r}\simeq{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{J}}^{\vee}_{C}}.

Hence

(17)

{\mathcal{L}}^{\otimes r}\simeq\mathrm{Norm}({\mathcal{F}}^{\prime}|_{D\times_{B}{\mathcal{J}}^{\vee}_{C}})^{\vee}\in\mathrm{Coh}({\mathcal{J}}^{\vee}_{C})_{(0)}.

The section in (
16
) also identifies
J^{d}_{C}
with
J^{0}_{C}
. Let

{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime}\in\mathrm{Coh}(J^{\vee}_{C}\times_{B}J^{d}_{C})

denote the pullback of the normalized Poincaré line bundle on
J^{\vee}_{C}\times_{B}J^{0}_{C}
via this identification. Then, by
[
35
, Corollary 4.5]
, we have

(18)

{\mathcal{P}}^{d}\simeq{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime}\otimes p_{{\mathcal{J}}^{\vee}_{C}}^{*}{\mathcal{L}}^{\otimes d}\in\mathrm{Coh}({\mathcal{J}}^{\vee}_{C}\times_{B}{\mathcal{J}}_{C}^{d})_{(d,0)}.

Applying
([N]\times\mathrm{id}_{{\mathcal{J}}^{d}_{C}})^{*}
on both sides, we find

\displaystyle([N]\times\mathrm{id}_{{\mathcal{J}}^{d}_{C}})^{*}{\mathcal{P}}^{d}

\displaystyle\simeq([N]\times\mathrm{id}_{{\mathcal{J}}^{d}_{C}})^{*}{\mathcal{P}}^{\prime}\otimes p^{*}_{{\mathcal{J}}^{\vee}_{C}}[N]^{*}{\mathcal{L}}^{\otimes d}

\displaystyle\simeq({\mathcal{P}}^{\prime})^{\otimes N}\otimes p^{*}_{{\mathcal{J}}^{\vee}_{C}}[N]^{*}{\mathcal{L}}^{\otimes d}\in\mathrm{Coh}({\mathcal{J}}^{\vee}_{C}\times_{B}{\mathcal{J}}^{d}_{C})_{(d,0)}

where the second isomorphism follows from properties of the normalized Poincaré line bundle. For comparison we also

({\mathcal{P}}^{d})^{\otimes N}\simeq({\mathcal{P}}^{\prime})^{\otimes N}\otimes p^{*}_{{\mathcal{J}}^{\vee}_{C}}({\mathcal{L}}^{\otimes d})^{\otimes N}\in\mathrm{Coh}({\mathcal{J}}^{\vee}_{C}\times_{B}{\mathcal{J}}^{d}_{C})_{(d,0)}.

Therefore, to prove the lemma it suffices to show

(19)

[N]^{*}c_{1}({\mathcal{L}})=Nc_{1}({\mathcal{L}})\in\mathrm{CH}^{1}(J^{\vee}_{C}).

This is a consequence of (
17
): in fact, viewing
{\mathcal{L}}^{\otimes r}
as an untwisted line bundle on the abelian scheme
\pi^{\vee}:J^{\vee}_{C}\to B
, we know from (
17
) that
{\mathcal{L}}^{\otimes r}
is fiberwise homologically trivial, and that the pullback of
{\mathcal{L}}^{\otimes r}
along the zero section
0:B\to J^{\vee}_{C}
is trivial.
Using the see-saw theorem
[
46
, II.8]
, this implies

[N]^{*}{\mathcal{L}}^{\otimes r}\simeq({\mathcal{L}}^{\otimes r})^{\otimes N}\in\mathrm{Coh}(J^{\vee}_{C})

and hence

[N]^{*}(rc_{1}({\mathcal{L}}))=Nrc_{1}({\mathcal{L}})\in\mathrm{CH}^{1}(J^{\vee}_{C}),

which gives (
19
) after dividing by
r
.
∎

Proof of Proposition
1.2
.

The second identity in (
11
) immediately implies for
k\neq 2g
,

\sum_{i+j=k}({\mathfrak{F}}^{d})^{-1}_{j}\circ{\mathfrak{F}}^{d}_{i}=0\in\mathrm{CH}^{k-g}(J_{C}^{\vee}\times_{B}J_{C}^{\vee}).

We now apply
([N]\times\mathrm{id}_{J^{\vee}_{C}})^{*}
to the identity above. For
N\equiv 1\pmod{r}
, we find

\displaystyle([N]\times\mathrm{id}_{J^{\vee}_{C}})^{*}\left(\sum_{i+j=k}({\mathfrak{F}}^{d})^{-1}_{j}\circ{\mathfrak{F}}^{d}_{i}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{i+j=k}({\mathfrak{F}}^{d})^{-1}_{j}\circ([N]\times\mathrm{id}_{J^{d}_{C}})^{*}{\mathrm{ch}}_{i}({\mathcal{P}}^{d})

\displaystyle=\sum_{i+j=k}({\mathfrak{F}}^{d})^{-1}_{j}\circ\frac{([N]\times\mathrm{id}_{J^{d}_{C}})^{*}c_{1}({\mathcal{P}}^{d})^{i}}{i!}

\displaystyle=\sum_{i+j=k}N^{i}({\mathfrak{F}}^{d})^{-1}_{j}\circ{\mathfrak{F}}^{d}_{i},

where the last equality uses Lemma
1.5
. Hence for
k\neq 2g
, we have

\sum_{i+j=k}N^{i}({\mathfrak{F}}^{d})^{-1}_{j}\circ{\mathfrak{F}}^{d}_{i}=0\in\mathrm{CH}^{k-g}(J_{C}^{\vee}\times_{B}J^{\vee}_{C})

for infinitely many integers
N
, which implies the vanishing of each individual term on the left-hand side.
∎

Proof of Proposition
1.3
.

We have

[\Delta_{J^{\vee}_{C}/B}]=\sum_{i=0}^{2g}{\mathfrak{p}}_{i}^{\vee},\quad([N]\times\mathrm{id}_{J^{\vee}_{C}})^{*}{\mathfrak{p}}_{i}^{\vee}=N^{2g-i}{\mathfrak{p}}_{i}^{\vee}

where the second identity follows from the previous proof. By
[
17
, Theorem 3.1]
, these identities already force the
{\mathfrak{p}}_{i}^{\vee}
to coincide with the
unique
pairwise orthogonal idempotents constructed by Deninger–Murre for the motivic decomposition of the abelian scheme
J_{C}^{\vee}
.
∎

1.3.
Convolution

For the smooth family
\pi_{d}:J^{d}_{C}\to B
, the convolution product

*^{d}:D^{b}\mathrm{Coh}({\mathcal{J}}_{C}^{\vee})_{(-d)}\times D^{b}\mathrm{Coh}({\mathcal{J}}_{C}^{\vee})_{(-d)}\to D^{b}\mathrm{Coh}({\mathcal{J}}_{C}^{\vee})_{(-d)}

is by definition the Fourier dual to the usual tensor product on
D^{b}\mathrm{Coh}({\mathcal{J}}_{C}^{d})_{(0)}
,
i.e.
,

F*^{d}G:=\mathrm{FM}_{({\mathcal{P}}^{d})^{-1}}(\mathrm{FM}_{{\mathcal{P}}^{d}}(F)\otimes\mathrm{FM}_{{\mathcal{P}}^{d}}(G)),\quad F,G\in D^{b}\mathrm{Coh}({\mathcal{J}}_{C}^{\vee})_{(-d)}.

In terms of Fourier–Mukai kernels, this corresponds to the convolution kernel

(20)

{\mathcal{K}}^{d}:=({\mathcal{P}}^{d})^{-1}\circ{\mathcal{O}}_{\Delta^{\mathrm{sm}}_{{\mathcal{J}}_{C}^{d}/B}}\circ({\mathcal{P}}^{d}\boxtimes{\mathcal{P}}^{d})\in D^{b}\mathrm{Coh}({\mathcal{J}}_{C}^{\vee}\times_{B}{\mathcal{J}}^{\vee}_{C}\times_{B}{\mathcal{J}}^{\vee}_{C})_{(d,d,-d)}.

Here
{\mathcal{O}}_{\Delta^{\mathrm{sm}}_{{\mathcal{J}}_{C}^{d}/B}}
is the structure sheaf of the
\mu_{r}^{\times 3}
-gerbe over the small relative diagonal

\Delta^{\mathrm{sm}}_{J^{d}_{C}/B}\subset J^{d}_{C}\times_{B}J^{d}_{C}\times_{B}J^{d}_{C}

which is the kernel for the tensor product on
D^{b}\mathrm{Coh}({\mathcal{J}}_{C}^{d})_{(0)}
, and again the subscript
(d,d,-d)
specifies the characters of the three
\mu_{r}
-actions.

Similarly, the Chow-theoretic convolution product is defined via the convolution class

(21)

{\mathfrak{C}}^{d}:=({\mathfrak{F}}^{d})^{-1}\circ[\Delta^{\mathrm{sm}}_{J^{d}_{C}/B}]\circ({\mathfrak{F}}^{d}\times{\mathfrak{F}}^{d})\in\mathrm{CH}^{*}(J^{\vee}_{C}\times_{B}J^{\vee}_{C}\times_{B}J^{\vee}_{C})

viewed as a correspondence between
J^{\vee}_{C}\times_{B}J^{\vee}_{C}
and the third factor
J^{\vee}_{C}
. Note that
{\mathfrak{C}}^{d}
is
a priori
both of mixed degree and dependent on
d
; however, the following proposition shows otherwise.

Proposition 1.6
.

For any integer
d
, the convolution class
{\mathfrak{C}}^{d}
in (
21
) is represented by the relative graph of the group law of the abelian scheme
\pi^{\vee}:J_{C}^{\vee}\to B
:

+:J^{\vee}_{C}\times_{B}J^{\vee}_{C}\to J^{\vee}_{C},\quad G_{+}\subset J^{\vee}_{C}\times_{B}J^{\vee}_{C}\times_{B}J^{\vee}_{C},

i.e.
, we have

{\mathfrak{C}}^{d}=[G_{+}]\in\mathrm{CH}^{g}(J^{\vee}_{C}\times_{B}J^{\vee}_{C}\times_{B}J^{\vee}_{C}).

In particular,
{\mathfrak{C}}^{d}
is pure of codimension
g
and is independent of
d
.

Assuming Proposition
1.6
, we may abbreviate
{\mathfrak{C}}^{d}
to
{\mathfrak{C}}
and derive some immediate cohomological consequences. Via the cycle class map

\mathrm{CH}^{i}(J^{\vee}_{C}\times_{B}J^{\vee}_{C}\times_{B}J^{\vee}_{C})\to H^{2i}(J^{\vee}_{C}\times_{B}J^{\vee}_{C}\times_{B}J^{\vee}_{C},{\mathbb{Q}})\\
\simeq\mathrm{Hom}_{D^{b}_{c}(B)}(\pi^{\vee}_{*}{\mathbb{Q}}_{J^{\vee}_{C}}\otimes\pi^{\vee}_{*}{\mathbb{Q}}_{J^{\vee}_{C}},\pi^{\vee}_{*}{\mathbb{Q}}_{J^{\vee}_{C}}[2i-4g])

the codimension
g
class
{\mathfrak{C}}
induces the convolution product

(22)

*:\pi^{\vee}_{*}{\mathbb{Q}}_{J^{\vee}_{C}}\otimes\pi^{\vee}_{*}{\mathbb{Q}}_{J^{\vee}_{C}}\to\pi^{\vee}_{*}{\mathbb{Q}}_{J^{\vee}_{C}}[-2g];

see
[
35
, Section 2.2.3]
. Similarly, the codimension
2g
class
[\Delta^{\mathrm{sm}}_{J^{d}_{C}/B}]
induces the usual cup product

\cup:\pi_{d*}{\mathbb{Q}}_{J^{d}_{C}}\otimes\pi_{d*}{\mathbb{Q}}_{J^{d}_{C}}\to\pi_{d*}{\mathbb{Q}}_{J^{d}_{C}}.

The two product structures are interchanged by the Fourier transform (
13
) and its inverse: we have a commutative diagram

(23)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi^{\vee}_{*}{\mathbb{Q}}_{J^{\vee}_{C}}\otimes\pi^{\vee}_{*}{\mathbb{Q}}_{J^{\vee}_{C}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi^{\vee}_{*}{\mathbb{Q}}_{J^{\vee}_{C}}[-2g]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{d*}{\mathbb{Q}}_{J^{d}_{C}}[-]\otimes\pi_{d*}{\mathbb{Q}}_{J^{d}_{C}}[-]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{d*}{\mathbb{Q}}_{J^{d}_{C}}[-].}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces*}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathfrak{F}}^{d}\otimes{\mathfrak{F}}^{d}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathfrak{F}}^{d}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cup}

In this diagram, the vertical arrows are a priori mixed-degree correspondences; we do not yet specify the shifts in the bottom row, which will become clearer in the next section. Taking global cohomology, we also obtain a commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{m}(J^{\vee}_{C},{\mathbb{Q}})\otimes H^{n}(J^{\vee}_{C},{\mathbb{Q}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{m+n-2g}(J^{\vee}_{C},{\mathbb{Q}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{*}(J^{d}_{C},{\mathbb{Q}})\otimes H^{*}(J^{d}_{C},{\mathbb{Q}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{*}(J^{d}_{C},{\mathbb{Q}}).}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces*}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathfrak{F}}^{d}\otimes{\mathfrak{F}}^{d}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathfrak{F}}^{d}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cup}

The proof of Proposition
1.6
follows the same lines as (FV) and traces back to properties of the convolution kernel (
20
).

Proof of Proposition
1.6
.

The statement for
d=0
is classical; see
[
30
, Theorem 1.3.1]
. We now show that the Chern character of the convolution kernel

{\mathrm{ch}}({\mathcal{K}}^{d})\in\mathrm{CH}^{*}(J^{\vee}_{C}\times_{B}J^{\vee}_{C}\times_{B}J^{\vee}_{C})

is independent of
d
. Since the relative Todd classes are pulled back from the base and are obviously independent of
d
, by applying Riemann–Roch we obtain the same statement for arbitrary
d
. As in the proof of Lemma
1.5
, up to a finite flat base change along
D\to B
we may assume that
C\to B
admits a section (
16
).

Let
{\mathcal{G}}_{+}
denote the
\mu_{r}^{\times 3}
-gerbe over the relative graph
G_{+}
of the group law of
\pi^{\vee}:J^{\vee}_{C}\to B
. For
d=0
, the Poincaré line bundle
{\mathcal{P}}^{0}
is untwisted, and Mukai’s original argument
[
45
, (3.7)]
shows that
{\mathcal{K}}^{0}
is just the structure sheaf
{\mathcal{O}}_{{\mathcal{G}}_{+}}
. More generally, we have by (
18
) that
{\mathcal{K}}^{d}
is the pushforward to
{\mathcal{J}}^{\vee}_{C}\times_{B}{\mathcal{J}}^{\vee}_{C}\times_{B}{\mathcal{J}}^{\vee}_{C}
of

p_{1}^{*}{\mathcal{L}}^{\otimes d}\otimes p_{2}^{*}{\mathcal{L}}^{\otimes d}\otimes p_{3}^{*}{\mathcal{L}}^{\otimes{(-d)}}\in\mathrm{Coh}({\mathcal{G}}_{+})_{(d,d,-d)}

where the
p_{i}:{\mathcal{G}}_{+}\to{\mathcal{J}}^{\vee}_{C}
are the three projections and the rest of the notation is as in the proof of Lemma
1.5
.

Therefore, by Riemann–Roch it suffices to show

(24)

c_{1}(p_{1}^{*}{\mathcal{L}}\otimes p_{2}^{*}{\mathcal{L}}\otimes p_{3}^{*}{\mathcal{L}}^{\vee})=0\in\mathrm{CH}^{1}(G_{+}).

Arguing as in the proof of Lemma
1.5
, we consider
{\mathcal{L}}^{\otimes r}
viewed as an untwisted line bundle on the abelian scheme
\pi^{\vee}:J^{\vee}_{C}\to B
. Since
{\mathcal{L}}^{\otimes r}
is fiberwise homologically trivial and pulls back trivially along the zero section
0:B\to J_{C}^{\vee}
, the see-saw theorem implies (see
[
46
, II.8]
)

+^{*}{\mathcal{L}}^{\otimes r}\simeq p_{1}^{*}{\mathcal{L}}^{\otimes r}\otimes p_{2}^{*}{\mathcal{L}}^{\otimes r}\in\mathrm{Coh}(J^{\vee}_{C}\times_{B}J^{\vee}_{C})

or equivalently

p_{1}^{*}{\mathcal{L}}^{\otimes r}\otimes p_{2}^{*}{\mathcal{L}}^{\otimes r}\otimes p_{3}^{*}{\mathcal{L}}^{\otimes(-r)}\simeq 0\in\mathrm{Coh}(G_{+}).

Hence

rc_{1}(p_{1}^{*}{\mathcal{L}}\otimes p_{2}^{*}{\mathcal{L}}\otimes p_{3}^{*}{\mathcal{L}}^{\vee})=0\in\mathrm{CH}^{1}(G_{+}),

which gives (
24
) after dividing by
r
.
∎

1.4.
Degree-independence

We are ready to state and prove our main theorem for Jacobian families of nonsingular curves.

Theorem 1.7
.

Let
\pi_{d}:J^{d}_{C}\to B
and
\pi^{\vee}:J^{\vee}_{C}\to B
be as in Section
1.1
, and recall the decompositions of the pushforward complexes from Proposition
1.4
.

(i)

(Strong Fourier-stability) For integers
i,j,k
with
0\leq i,j\leq 2g
, the restriction–projection of
{\mathfrak{F}}^{d}_{k}
to

{\mathfrak{F}}^{d}_{k}:(\pi_{*}^{\vee}{\mathbb{Q}}_{J_{C}^{\vee}})_{(i)}\to(\pi_{d*}{\mathbb{Q}}_{J_{C}^{d}})_{(j)}[2k-2g]

is nonzero only if
j=k=2g-i
. Similarly, the restriction–projection of
({\mathfrak{F}}^{d})^{-1}_{k}
to

({\mathfrak{F}}^{d})^{-1}_{k}:(\pi_{d*}{\mathbb{Q}}_{J_{C}^{d}})_{(i)}\to(\pi_{*}^{\vee}{\mathbb{Q}}_{J_{C}^{\vee}})_{(j)}[2k-2g]

is nonzero only if
j=k=2g-i
. There are mutually inverse isomorphisms

(\pi_{*}^{\vee}{\mathbb{Q}}_{J_{C}^{\vee}})_{(2g-i)}[2g-2i]\xrightleftharpoons[({\mathfrak{F}}^{d})_{2g-i}^{-1}]{{\mathfrak{F}}^{d}_{i}}(\pi_{d*}{\mathbb{Q}}_{J_{C}^{d}})_{(i)}.

(ii)

(Strong multiplicativity) For
0\leq i,j,k\leq 2g
, the restriction–projection of the cup-product
\cup
to

\cup:(\pi_{d*}{\mathbb{Q}}_{J_{C}^{d}})_{(i)}\otimes(\pi_{d*}{\mathbb{Q}}_{J_{C}^{d}})_{(j)}\to(\pi_{d*}{\mathbb{Q}}_{J_{C}^{d}})_{(k)}

is nonzero only if
k=i+j
.

(iii)

(Degree-independence) For integers
d,d^{\prime}
, there is an isomorphism

\pi_{d*}{\mathbb{Q}}_{J_{C}^{d}}\simeq\pi_{d^{\prime}*}{\mathbb{Q}}_{J_{C}^{d^{\prime}}}\in D^{b}_{c}(B)

which is compatible with the decompositions (as in Proposition
1.4
) and the cup-products on both sides.

Proof.

By definition, the first restriction–projection in (i) is induced by the Chow correspondence

(25)

{\mathfrak{p}}^{d}_{j}\circ{\mathfrak{F}}^{d}_{k}\circ{\mathfrak{p}}^{\vee}_{i}={\mathfrak{F}}^{d}_{j}\circ({\mathfrak{F}}^{d})^{-1}_{2g-j}\circ{\mathfrak{F}}^{d}_{k}\circ{\mathfrak{p}}^{\vee}_{i}.

Applying (FV), we see that (
25
) is nonzero only if
j=k
, in which case we find

{\mathfrak{F}}^{d}_{j}\circ({\mathfrak{F}}^{d})^{-1}_{2g-j}\circ{\mathfrak{F}}^{d}_{j}\circ{\mathfrak{p}}^{\vee}_{i}={\mathfrak{F}}^{d}_{j}\circ{\mathfrak{p}}^{\vee}_{2g-j}\circ{\mathfrak{p}}^{\vee}_{i}.

The pairwise orthogonality of the
{\mathfrak{p}}_{i}^{\vee}
from Proposition
1.3
shows that (
25
) is nonzero only if
j=k=2g-i
. The second restriction–projection comes from the Chow correspondence

{\mathfrak{p}}^{\vee}_{j}\circ({\mathfrak{F}}^{d})^{-1}_{k}\circ{\mathfrak{p}}^{d}_{i}={\mathfrak{p}}^{\vee}_{j}\circ({\mathfrak{F}}^{d})^{-1}_{k}\circ{\mathfrak{F}}^{d}_{i}\circ({\mathfrak{F}}^{d})_{2g-i}^{-1}

which for the same reason is nonzero only if
j=k=2g-i
. The third statement in (i) follows from the first two together with the identities (
14
).

For (ii), we perform Fourier transforms as in (
23
), and apply (i) to get a commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\pi^{\vee}_{*}{\mathbb{Q}}_{J^{\vee}_{C}})_{(2g-i)}[2g-2i]\otimes(\pi^{\vee}_{*}{\mathbb{Q}}_{J^{\vee}_{C}})_{(2g-j)}[2g-2j]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\pi^{\vee}_{*}{\mathbb{Q}}_{J^{\vee}_{C}})_{(2g-k)}[2g-2k]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\pi_{d*}{\mathbb{Q}}_{J^{d}_{C}})_{(i)}\otimes(\pi_{d*}{\mathbb{Q}}_{J^{d}_{C}})_{(j)}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces(\pi_{d*}{\mathbb{Q}}_{J^{d}_{C}})_{(k)}.}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces*}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathfrak{F}}^{d}_{i}\otimes{\mathfrak{F}}^{d}_{j}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathfrak{F}}^{d}_{k}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\simeq}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cup}

Looking at the top row and comparing with the shift in (
22
), we see that for the bottom row to be nonzero we must have

2g-2k-(2g-i)-(2g-j)=-2g,

i.e.
,
k=i+j
.

Finally, for any integer
d
, we have by (i) mutually inverse graded isomorphisms

\bigoplus_{i=0}^{2g}(\pi_{*}^{\vee}{\mathbb{Q}}_{J_{C}^{\vee}})_{(2g-i)}[2g-2i]\xrightleftharpoons[\oplus({\mathfrak{F}}^{d})_{2g-i}^{-1}]{\oplus{\mathfrak{F}}^{d}_{i}}\bigoplus_{i=0}^{2g}(\pi_{d*}{\mathbb{Q}}_{J_{C}^{d}})_{(i)}.

Moreover, by (ii) the above isomorphisms are compatible with the convolution product on the left-hand side and the cup-product on the right-hand side. This proves (iii) as both the decomposition and the convolution product on the left-hand side are independent of
d
, by Propositions
1.3
and
1.6
. The proof of Theorem
1.7
is now complete.
∎

The following corollary is immediate after taking global cohomology.

Corollary 1.8
.

For any integer
d
, the cohomology

H^{*}(J^{d}_{C},{\mathbb{Q}})=\bigoplus_{i,m}H^{m}(J^{d}_{C},{\mathbb{Q}})_{(i)}

is a bigraded
H^{*}(B,{\mathbb{Q}})
-algebra. For integers
d,d^{\prime}
, there is an isomorphism

H^{*}(J^{d}_{C},{\mathbb{Q}})\simeq H^{*}(J^{d^{\prime}}_{C},{\mathbb{Q}})

of bigraded
H^{*}(B,{\mathbb{Q}})
-algebras. ∎

Remark 1.9
.

(i)

So far our argument has been purely Chow-theoretic; the same argument would prove that the Chow ring
\mathrm{CH}^{*}(J^{d}_{C})
is, as a bigraded
\mathrm{CH}^{*}(B)
-algebra, independent of
d
.

(ii)

Although we assumed the base
B
to be a nonsingular quasiprojective variety, Theorem
1.7
and Corollary
1.8
hold equally for the degree
d
universal Jacobian

\pi_{d}:J^{d}_{g}\to{\mathcal{M}}_{g}

over the moduli stack of nonsingular curves of genus
g
. The quickest way is to add a level
\ell\geq 3
structure to obtain a finite Galois cover of
{\mathcal{M}}_{g}
by a nonsingular quasiprojective variety
B
. The multisection
D
in (
8
) is chosen to be invariant under the
\mathrm{Sp}_{2g}({\mathbb{Z}}/\ell{\mathbb{Z}})
-action. The rest of the argument is obviously independent of the level structure.

2.
Compactified Jacobians

We proceed to integral locally planar curves and their compactified Jacobians. We begin by reviewing results in
[
35
]
which provide a new characterization of the perverse filtration on the cohomology of a compactified Jacobian family and establish its multiplicativity. We then prove that the associated graded with respect to the perverse filtration has a ring structure which is independent of the degree of the compactified Jacobian; see Theorem
2.6
and Corollary
2.7
.

2.1.
Perverse filtration

Let
C\to B
be a flat projective family of integral locally planar curves of arithmetic genus
g
over a nonsingular quasiprojective base variety
B
, and for any integer
d
, let

\pi_{d}:\overline{J}^{d}_{C}\to B

be the associated compactifed Jacobian family parameterizing degree
d
rank
1
torsion-free sheaves. We assume that the total space
\overline{J}^{d}_{C}
is nonsingular. Of particular interest is the case
B=\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g}^{\mathrm{int}}
,
i.e.
, the moduli stack of integral stable curves of genus
g
. The reason for treating
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g}^{\mathrm{int}}
as a nonsingular quasiprojective variety is the same as in Remark
1.9
(ii), where one replaces the level
\ell
structure by the level structures constructed in
[
1
]
for all of
\overline{\mathcal{M}}_{g,n}
.

The Beilinson–Bernstein–Deligne–Gabber decomposition theorem
[
10
]
, applied to the proper map
\pi_{d}:\overline{J}^{d}_{C}\to B
and the constant sheaf
{\mathbb{Q}}_{\overline{J}_{C}^{d}}
, yields a
non-canonical
decomposition

(26)

\pi_{d*}{\mathbb{Q}}_{\overline{J}_{C}^{d}}\simeq\bigoplus_{k=0}^{2g}{\mathcal{H}}^{d}_{(k)},\quad{\mathcal{H}}^{d}_{(k)}:={{}^{\mathfrak{p}}}H^{k+\dim B}(\pi_{d*}{\mathbb{Q}}_{\overline{J}_{C}^{d}})[-k-\dim B]\in D^{b}_{c}(B).

Here
{{}^{\mathfrak{p}}}H^{i}(-)
is the
i
-th perverse cohomology functor. What remain canonical are the perverse truncation functors
{{}^{\mathfrak{p}}}\tau_{\leq\bullet}(-),{{}^{\mathfrak{p}}}\tau_{\geq\bullet}(-)
applied to the pushforward complex
\pi_{d*}{\mathbb{Q}}_{\overline{J}_{C}^{d}}
: for
0\leq k\leq 2g
, there are natural distinguished triangles

\displaystyle{{}^{\mathfrak{p}}}\tau_{\leq k+\dim B}(\pi_{d*}{\mathbb{Q}}_{\overline{J}_{C}^{d}})\to\pi_{d*}{\mathbb{Q}}_{\overline{J}_{C}^{d}}\to{{}^{\mathfrak{p}}}\tau_{\geq k+1+\dim B}(\pi_{d*}{\mathbb{Q}}_{\overline{J}_{C}^{d}})\xrightarrow{+1},

(27)

\displaystyle{{}^{\mathfrak{p}}}\tau_{\leq k-1+\dim B}(\pi_{d*}{\mathbb{Q}}_{\overline{J}_{C}^{d}})\to{{}^{\mathfrak{p}}}\tau_{\leq k+\dim B}(\pi_{d*}{\mathbb{Q}}_{\overline{J}_{C}^{d}})\to{\mathcal{H}}^{d}_{(k)}\xrightarrow{+1}.

Taking global cohomology and setting

P_{k}H^{*}(\overline{J}^{d}_{C},{\mathbb{Q}}):=\mathrm{Im}\left(H^{*}(B,{{}^{\mathfrak{p}}}\tau_{\leq k+\dim B}(\pi_{d*}{\mathbb{Q}}_{\overline{J}_{C}^{d}}))\to H^{*}(B,\pi_{d*}{\mathbb{Q}}_{\overline{J}_{C}^{d}})=H^{*}(\overline{J}_{C}^{d},{\mathbb{Q}})\right),

we obtain the perverse filtration

P_{0}H^{*}(\overline{J}^{d}_{C},{\mathbb{Q}})\subset P_{1}H^{*}(\overline{J}^{d}_{C},{\mathbb{Q}})\subset\cdots\subset P_{2g}H^{*}(\overline{J}^{d}_{C},{\mathbb{Q}})=H^{*}(\overline{J}^{d}_{C},{\mathbb{Q}})

and the canonical identification

\mathrm{Gr}_{k}^{P}H^{*}(\overline{J}_{C}^{d},{\mathbb{Q}})=H^{*}(B,{\mathcal{H}}^{d}_{(k)}).

2.2.
Perverse filtration via Fourier

A new characterization of the perverse filtration
P_{\bullet}H^{*}(\overline{J}_{C}^{d},{\mathbb{Q}})
(as well as its sheaf-theoretic counterpart) was given in
[
35
]
using Fourier transforms. We review the construction and key results needed for the main Theorem
2.6
. These results will be axiomatized and extended to more general situations in Section
3
.

As in the nonsingular case we fix once and for all

\overline{J}^{\vee}_{C}:=\overline{J}^{0}_{C},\quad\pi^{\vee}:=\pi_{0}:\overline{J}^{\vee}_{C}\to B.

The perverse truncations
{{}^{\mathfrak{p}}}\tau_{\leq\bullet}(\pi^{\vee}_{*}{\mathbb{Q}}_{\overline{J}^{\vee}_{C}}),{{}^{\mathfrak{p}}}\tau_{\geq\bullet}(\pi^{\vee}_{*}{\mathbb{Q}}_{\overline{J}^{\vee}_{C}})
, the shifted perverse sheaves
{\mathcal{H}}^{\vee}_{(\bullet)}
, and the perverse filtration
P_{\bullet}H^{*}(\overline{J}^{\vee}_{C},{\mathbb{Q}})
are defined accordingly.

By considering families of rank
1
torsion-free sheaves trivialized along an
r
-fold multisection

D\subset C\to B,

whose existence follows again from
[
14
, Theorem 1.1]
, and by applying Arinkin’s construction
[
3
]
, we obtain a Poincaré sheaf

{\mathcal{P}}^{d}\in\mathrm{Coh}(\overline{\mathcal{J}}^{\vee}_{C}\times\overline{\mathcal{J}}^{d}_{C})_{(d,0)}

on the relative product of certain
\mu_{r}
-gerbes
\overline{\mathcal{J}}^{\vee}_{C},\overline{\mathcal{J}}^{d}_{C}
over
\overline{J}^{\vee}_{C},\overline{J}^{d}_{C}
; see
[
35
, Proposition 4.3]
. We then use singular Riemann–Roch for quotient stacks
[
18
,
19
]
to defined the Chow-theoretic Fourier transform

{\mathfrak{F}}^{d}=\sum_{i}{\mathfrak{F}}^{d}_{i}\in\mathrm{CH}_{*}(\overline{J}_{C}^{\vee}\times_{B}\overline{J}^{d}_{C}),\quad{\mathfrak{F}}^{d}_{i}\in\mathrm{CH}_{2g-i+\dim B}(\overline{J}_{C}^{\vee}\times_{B}\overline{J}^{d}_{C})

and its inverse

({\mathfrak{F}}^{d})^{-1}=\sum_{i}({\mathfrak{F}}^{d})^{-1}_{i}\in\mathrm{CH}_{*}(\overline{J}_{C}^{d}\times_{B}\overline{J}^{\vee}_{C}),\quad({\mathfrak{F}}^{d})^{-1}_{i}\in\mathrm{CH}_{2g-i+\dim B}(\overline{J}_{C}^{d}\times_{B}\overline{J}^{\vee}_{C});

see
[
35
, Sections 2.4 and 4.4]
. Further, under the cycle class maps

\displaystyle\begin{multlined}\mathrm{CH}_{2g-i+\dim B}(\overline{J}_{C}^{\vee}\times_{B}\overline{J}^{d}_{C})\to H^{\mathrm{BM}}_{4g-2i+2\dim B}(\overline{J}_{C}^{\vee}\times_{B}\overline{J}^{d}_{C},{\mathbb{Q}})\\
\simeq\operatorname{Hom}_{D^{b}_{c}(B)}(\pi^{\vee}_{*}{\mathbb{Q}}_{\overline{J}_{C}^{\vee}},\pi_{d*}{\mathbb{Q}}_{\overline{J}_{C}^{d}}[2i-2g]),\end{multlined}

\displaystyle\begin{multlined}\mathrm{CH}_{2g-i+\dim B}(\overline{J}_{C}^{d}\times_{B}\overline{J}_{C}^{\vee})\to H^{\mathrm{BM}}_{4g-2i+2\dim B}(\overline{J}_{C}^{d}\times_{B}\overline{J}^{\vee}_{C},{\mathbb{Q}})\\
\simeq\operatorname{Hom}_{D^{b}_{c}(B)}(\pi_{d*}{\mathbb{Q}}_{\overline{J}_{C}^{d}},\pi^{\vee}_{*}{\mathbb{Q}}_{\overline{J}_{C}^{\vee}}[2i-2g]),\end{multlined}

we obtain the sheaf-theoretic Fourier transform and its inverse

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{F}}^{d}=\sum_{i}{\mathfrak{F}}^{d}_{i}:\pi^{\vee}_{*}{\mathbb{Q}}_{\overline{J}_{C}^{\vee}}\to\pi_{d*}{\mathbb{Q}}_{\overline{J}_{C}^{d}}[-],\quad{\mathfrak{F}}^{d}_{i}:\pi^{\vee}_{*}{\mathbb{Q}}_{\overline{J}_{C}^{\vee}}\to\pi_{d*}{\mathbb{Q}}_{\overline{J}_{C}^{d}}[2i-2g]

\displaystyle({\mathfrak{F}}^{d})^{-1}=\sum_{i}({\mathfrak{F}}^{d})^{-1}_{i}:\pi_{d*}{\mathbb{Q}}_{\overline{J}_{C}^{d}}\to\pi^{\vee}_{*}{\mathbb{Q}}_{\overline{J}_{C}^{\vee}}[-],\quad({\mathfrak{F}}^{d})^{-1}_{i}:\pi_{d*}{\mathbb{Q}}_{\overline{J}_{C}^{d}}\to\pi^{\vee}_{*}{\mathbb{Q}}_{\overline{J}_{C}^{\vee}}[2i-2g];

see
[
35
, Section 2.2.2]
.

The starting point is the following weaker version of the Fourier vanishing, as compared with Proposition
1.2
.

Proposition 2.1

(
\frac{1}{2}
Fourier vanishing
[
35
, Sections 3.5 and 4.4]
)
.

For all
i+j<2g
, we have

(
\frac{1}{2}
FV1)

\displaystyle({\mathfrak{F}}^{d})^{-1}_{j}\circ{\mathfrak{F}}^{d}_{i}=0\in\mathrm{CH}_{3g-i-j+\dim B}(\overline{J}_{C}^{\vee}\times_{B}\overline{J}_{C}^{\vee}),

(
\frac{1}{2}
FV2)

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{F}}^{d}_{j}\circ({\mathfrak{F}}^{d})^{-1}_{i}=0\in\mathrm{CH}_{3g-i-j+\dim B}(\overline{J}_{C}^{d}\times_{B}\overline{J}_{C}^{d}).

Note that only (
\frac{1}{2}
FV1) was stated and used in
[
35
]
. Here for our purpose we shall need (
\frac{1}{2}
FV2) whose proof is identical to that of (
\frac{1}{2}
FV1); see also
[
36
, Proposition 2.9]
.

We define for
0\leq k\leq 2g
Chow self-correspondences

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{p}}^{d}_{\leq k}:=\sum_{i\leq k}{\mathfrak{F}}^{d}_{i}\circ({\mathfrak{F}}^{d})^{-1}_{2g-i}\in\mathrm{CH}_{g+\dim B}(\overline{J}_{C}^{d}\times_{B}\overline{J}^{d}_{C}),

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{p}}^{\vee}_{\leq k}:=\sum_{i\leq k}({\mathfrak{F}}^{d})^{-1}_{i}\circ{\mathfrak{F}}^{d}_{2g-i}\in\mathrm{CH}_{g+\dim B}(\overline{J}^{\vee}_{C}\times_{B}\overline{J}^{\vee}_{C}).

With half of the Fourier vanishing (
\frac{1}{2}
FV1) (resp. (
\frac{1}{2}
FV2)) we can only conclude that the
{\mathfrak{p}}^{d}_{\leq k}
(resp.
{\mathfrak{p}}^{\vee}_{\leq k}
) are
semi-orthogonal
idempotents,
i.e.
,

{\mathfrak{p}}^{d}_{\leq l}\circ{\mathfrak{p}}^{d}_{\leq k}={\mathfrak{p}}^{d}_{\leq k},\quad{\mathfrak{p}}^{\vee}_{\leq l}\circ{\mathfrak{p}}^{\vee}_{\leq k}={\mathfrak{p}}^{\vee}_{\leq k},\quad k\leq l.

The same statements hold for the induced sheaf-theoretic idempotents

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{p}}^{d}_{\leq k}:=\sum_{i\leq k}{\mathfrak{F}}^{d}_{i}\circ({\mathfrak{F}}^{d})^{-1}_{2g-i}:\pi_{d*}{\mathbb{Q}}_{\overline{J}_{C}^{d}}\to\pi_{d*}{\mathbb{Q}}_{\overline{J}_{C}^{d}},

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{p}}^{\vee}_{\leq k}:=\sum_{i\leq k}({\mathfrak{F}}^{d})^{-1}_{i}\circ{\mathfrak{F}}^{d}_{2g-i}:\pi^{\vee}_{*}{\mathbb{Q}}_{\overline{J}_{C}^{\vee}}\to\pi^{\vee}_{*}{\mathbb{Q}}_{\overline{J}_{C}^{\vee}}.

Again, we can talk about the image of the sheaf-theoretic
{\mathfrak{p}}^{d}_{\leq k}
(resp.
{\mathfrak{p}}^{\vee}_{\leq k}
) since the category
D^{b}_{c}(B)
is pseudo-abelian
[
15
, Lemma 2.24]
. The following result crucially relies on the
full support
property of the pushforward complexes
\pi_{d*}{\mathbb{Q}}_{\overline{J}_{C}^{d}}
and
\pi^{\vee}_{*}{\mathbb{Q}}_{\overline{J}_{C}^{\vee}}
; we will come back to this point later in Section
3
.

Theorem 2.2

(Realization
[
35
, Corollary 4.6(ii)]
)
.

For
0\leq k\leq 2g
, the natural inclusion
\mathrm{Im}({\mathfrak{p}}^{d}_{\leq k})\to\pi_{d*}{\mathbb{Q}}_{\overline{J}_{C}^{d}}
realizes the perverse truncation

{{}^{\mathfrak{p}}}\tau_{\leq k+\dim B}(\pi_{d*}{\mathbb{Q}}_{\overline{J}_{C}^{d}})\to\pi_{d*}{\mathbb{Q}}_{\overline{J}_{C}^{d}}

and provides a non-canonical splitting

(28)

\pi_{d*}{\mathbb{Q}}_{\overline{J}_{C}^{d}}\simeq{{}^{\mathfrak{p}}}\tau_{\leq k+\dim B}(\pi_{d*}{\mathbb{Q}}_{\overline{J}_{C}^{d}})\oplus{{}^{\mathfrak{p}}}\tau_{\geq k+1+\dim B}(\pi_{d*}{\mathbb{Q}}_{\overline{J}_{C}^{d}}).

Similarly, the inclusion
\mathrm{Im}({\mathfrak{p}}^{\vee}_{\leq k})\to\pi^{\vee}_{*}{\mathbb{Q}}_{\overline{J}_{C}^{\vee}}
realizes the perverse truncation

{{}^{\mathfrak{p}}}\tau_{\leq k+\dim B}(\pi^{\vee}_{*}{\mathbb{Q}}_{\overline{J}_{C}^{\vee}})\to\pi^{\vee}_{*}{\mathbb{Q}}_{\overline{J}_{C}^{\vee}}

and provides a non-canonical splitting

(29)

\pi^{\vee}_{*}{\mathbb{Q}}_{\overline{J}_{C}^{\vee}}\simeq{{}^{\mathfrak{p}}}\tau_{\leq k+\dim B}(\pi^{\vee}_{*}{\mathbb{Q}}_{\overline{J}_{C}^{\vee}})\oplus{{}^{\mathfrak{p}}}\tau_{\geq k+1+\dim B}(\pi^{\vee}_{*}{\mathbb{Q}}_{\overline{J}_{C}^{\vee}}).

Here the splitting (
28
) (resp. (
29
)) is given by the image of

(30)

{\mathfrak{q}}^{d}_{\geq k+1}:=\mathrm{id}_{\pi_{d*}{\mathbb{Q}}_{\overline{J}_{C}^{d}}}-{\mathfrak{p}}^{d}_{\leq k}=\sum_{i\geq k+1}{\mathfrak{F}}^{d}_{i}\circ({\mathfrak{F}}^{d})^{-1}_{2g-i}:\pi_{d*}{\mathbb{Q}}_{\overline{J}_{C}^{d}}\to\pi_{d*}{\mathbb{Q}}_{\overline{J}_{C}^{d}},

and respectively,

(31)

{\mathfrak{q}}^{\vee}_{\geq k+1}:=\mathrm{id}_{\pi^{\vee}_{*}{\mathbb{Q}}_{\overline{J}_{C}^{\vee}}}-{\mathfrak{p}}^{\vee}_{\leq k}=\sum_{i\geq k+1}({\mathfrak{F}}^{d})^{-1}_{i}\circ{\mathfrak{F}}^{d}_{2g-i}:\pi^{\vee}_{*}{\mathbb{Q}}_{\overline{J}_{C}^{\vee}}\to\pi^{\vee}_{*}{\mathbb{Q}}_{\overline{J}_{C}^{\vee}}.

Remark 2.3
.

Unlike in Proposition
1.3
, this time we do
not
claim the Chow (or sheaf-theoretic) idempotents
{\mathfrak{p}}^{\vee}_{\leq k}
to be independent of the degree
d
. Each image
\mathrm{Im}({\mathfrak{p}}^{\vee}_{\leq k})\in D^{b}_{c}(B)
, on the other hand, is canonically isomorphic to
{{}^{\mathfrak{p}}}\tau_{\leq k+\dim B}(\pi^{\vee}_{*}{\mathbb{Q}}_{\overline{J}_{C}^{\vee}})
and hence independent of
d
.

An important outcome of Theorem
2.2
is the multiplicativity of the perverse filtration
P_{\bullet}H^{*}(\overline{J}_{C}^{d},{\mathbb{Q}})
. There is also a stronger, sheaf-theoretic version concerning the cup-product

(32)

\cup:\pi_{d*}{\mathbb{Q}}_{\overline{J}_{C}^{d}}\otimes\pi_{d*}{\mathbb{Q}}_{\overline{J}_{C}^{d}}\to\pi_{d*}{\mathbb{Q}}_{\overline{J}_{C}^{d}}.

Theorem 2.4

(Multiplicativity
[
35
, Corollary 4.6(iii)]
)
.

For integers
k,l
, the perverse truncation

\cup:{{}^{\mathfrak{p}}}\tau_{\leq k+\dim B}(\pi_{d*}{\mathbb{Q}}_{\overline{J}_{C}^{d}})\otimes{{}^{\mathfrak{p}}}\tau_{\leq l+\dim B}(\pi_{d*}{\mathbb{Q}}_{\overline{J}_{C}^{d}})\to\pi_{d*}{\mathbb{Q}}_{\overline{J}_{C}^{d}}.

of the cup-product in (
32
) factors through

\cup:{{}^{\mathfrak{p}}}\tau_{\leq k+\dim B}(\pi_{d*}{\mathbb{Q}}_{\overline{J}_{C}^{d}})\otimes{{}^{\mathfrak{p}}}\tau_{\leq l+\dim B}(\pi_{d*}{\mathbb{Q}}_{\overline{J}_{C}^{d}})\to{{}^{\mathfrak{p}}}\tau_{\leq k+l+\dim B}(\pi_{d*}{\mathbb{Q}}_{\overline{J}_{C}^{d}}).

In other words, the composition

{{}^{\mathfrak{p}}}\tau_{\leq k+\dim B}(\pi_{d*}{\mathbb{Q}}_{\overline{J}_{C}^{d}})\otimes{{}^{\mathfrak{p}}}\tau_{\leq l+\dim B}(\pi_{d*}{\mathbb{Q}}_{\overline{J}_{C}^{d}})\to\pi_{d*}{\mathbb{Q}}_{\overline{J}_{C}^{d}}\otimes\pi_{d*}{\mathbb{Q}}_{\overline{J}_{C}^{d}}\xrightarrow{\cup}\pi_{d*}{\mathbb{Q}}_{\overline{J}_{C}^{d}}\\
\to\tau_{\geq k+l+1+\dim B}(\pi_{d*}{\mathbb{Q}}_{\overline{J}_{C}^{d}})

is zero.

As a result, the cup-product naturally descends via (
27
) to a collection of morphisms

(33)

\overline{\cup}:{\mathcal{H}}^{d}_{(k)}\otimes{\mathcal{H}}^{d}_{(l)}\to{\mathcal{H}}^{d}_{(k+l)}.

Setting the sheaf-theoretic associated graded

(34)

{\mathcal{H}}^{d}:=\bigoplus_{k=0}^{2g}{\mathcal{H}}^{d}_{(k)}\in D^{b}_{c}(B),

we obtain a cup-product

(35)

\overline{\cup}:{\mathcal{H}}^{d}\otimes{\mathcal{H}}^{d}\to{\mathcal{H}}^{d}

making
{\mathcal{H}}^{d}
a graded ring object in
D^{b}_{c}(B)
. Note however that (
32
) and (
35
) are in general
not
compatible with the non-canonical isomorphism (
26
).

The proof of Theorem
2.4
again involves the convolution product defined via the Chow class

{\mathfrak{C}}^{d}:=({\mathfrak{F}}^{d})^{-1}\circ[\Delta^{\mathrm{sm}}_{\overline{J}^{d}_{C}/B}]\circ({\mathfrak{F}}^{d}\times{\mathfrak{F}}^{d})\in\mathrm{CH}_{*}(\overline{J}^{\vee}_{C}\times_{B}\overline{J}^{\vee}_{C}\times_{B}\overline{J}^{\vee}_{C}),

where
\Delta^{\mathrm{sm}}_{\overline{J}^{d}_{C}/B}\subset\overline{J}^{d}_{C}\times_{B}\overline{J}^{d}_{C}\times_{B}\overline{J}^{d}_{C}
is the relative small diagonal responsible for the cup-product (
32
). A key step in establishing Theorem
2.4
is a dimension bound

(36)

{\mathfrak{C}}^{d}\in\mathrm{CH}_{\leq 2g+\dim B}(\overline{J}^{\vee}_{C}\times_{B}\overline{J}^{\vee}_{C}\times_{B}\overline{J}^{\vee}_{C})

which is weaker than the pure codimension
g
statement in Proposition
1.6
. The proof of the dimension bound further traces back to the support of the convolution kernel

(37)

{\mathcal{K}}^{d}:=({\mathcal{P}}^{d})^{-1}\circ{\mathcal{O}}_{\Delta^{\mathrm{sm}}_{\overline{{\mathcal{J}}}_{C}^{d}/B}}\circ({\mathcal{P}}^{d}\boxtimes{\mathcal{P}}^{d})\in D^{b}\mathrm{Coh}(\overline{{\mathcal{J}}}_{C}^{\vee}\times_{B}\overline{{\mathcal{J}}}^{\vee}_{C}\times_{B}\overline{{\mathcal{J}}}^{\vee}_{C})_{(d,d,-d)}.

Here
{\mathcal{O}}_{\Delta^{\mathrm{sm}}_{\overline{{\mathcal{J}}}_{C}^{d}/B}}
is the structure sheaf of the
\mu_{r}^{\times 3}
-gerbe over
\Delta^{\mathrm{sm}}_{\overline{J}^{d}_{C}/B}
; see
[
35
, Section 4.4]
.

Via the cycle class map

\mathrm{CH}_{\leq 2g+\dim B}(\overline{J}^{\vee}_{C}\times_{B}\overline{J}^{\vee}_{C}\times_{B}\overline{J}^{\vee}_{C})\to H^{\mathrm{BM}}_{\leq 4g+2\dim B}(\overline{J}^{\vee}_{C}\times_{B}\overline{J}^{\vee}_{C}\times_{B}\overline{J}^{\vee}_{C},{\mathbb{Q}})\\
\simeq\mathrm{Hom}_{D^{b}_{c}(B)}(\pi^{\vee}_{*}{\mathbb{Q}}_{\overline{J}^{\vee}_{C}}\otimes\pi^{\vee}_{*}{\mathbb{Q}}_{\overline{J}^{\vee}_{C}},\pi^{\vee}_{*}{\mathbb{Q}}_{\overline{J}^{\vee}_{C}}[\geq\!{-2g}])

the class
{\mathfrak{C}}^{d}
induces the sheaf-theoretic convolution product

*^{d}:\pi^{\vee}_{*}{\mathbb{Q}}_{J^{\vee}_{C}}\otimes\pi^{\vee}_{*}{\mathbb{Q}}_{J^{\vee}_{C}}\to\pi^{\vee}_{*}{\mathbb{Q}}_{J^{\vee}_{C}}[\geq\!{-2g}];

see
[
35
, Section 2.2.3]
. By definition, there is a commutative diagram interchanging the two product structures

(38)

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi^{\vee}_{*}{\mathbb{Q}}_{\overline{J}^{\vee}_{C}}\otimes\pi^{\vee}_{*}{\mathbb{Q}}_{\overline{J}^{\vee}_{C}}}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi^{\vee}_{*}{\mathbb{Q}}_{\overline{J}^{\vee}_{C}}[\geq\!-2g]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{d*}{\mathbb{Q}}_{\overline{J}^{d}_{C}}[-]\otimes\pi_{d*}{\mathbb{Q}}_{\overline{J}^{d}_{C}}[-]}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\pi_{d*}{\mathbb{Q}}_{\overline{J}^{d}_{C}}[-].}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces*^{d}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathfrak{F}}^{d}\otimes{\mathfrak{F}}^{d}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathfrak{F}}^{d}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cup}

We are particular interested in the lowest codimension (
=\!g
) component of
{\mathfrak{C}}^{d}
in (
36
); we call it the
reduced
convolution class

(39)

{\mathfrak{C}}^{d}_{\mathrm{red}}\in\mathrm{CH}_{2g+\dim B}(\overline{J}^{\vee}_{C}\times_{B}\overline{J}^{\vee}_{C}\times_{B}\overline{J}^{\vee}_{C}).

The following proposition is crucial to the proof of the degree-independence Theorem
2.6
(iii).

Proposition 2.5
.

The reduced convolution class
{\mathfrak{C}}^{d}_{\mathrm{red}}
in (
39
) is independent of the degree
d
.

Proof.

Since the convolution kernel
{\mathcal{K}}^{d}
in (
37
) is shown in
[
35
, Proposition 3.2 and Section 4.4]
to be supported in codimension
g
, the component
{\mathfrak{C}}^{d}_{\mathrm{red}}
is simply given by the fundamental class of the codimension
g
support of
{\mathcal{K}}^{d}
. On the other hand,
[
35
, Corollary 4.5]
shows that étale locally the
{\mathcal{K}}^{d}
only differ by tensoring a line bundle, and hence have the same support. In particular, the class
{\mathfrak{C}}^{d}_{\mathrm{red}}
is independent of
d
.
∎

Therefore,
{\mathfrak{C}}^{d}_{\mathrm{red}}
can be abbreviated to
{\mathfrak{C}}_{\mathrm{red}}
, and induces a reduced convolution product

(40)

*_{\mathrm{red}}:\pi^{\vee}_{*}{\mathbb{Q}}_{J^{\vee}_{C}}\otimes\pi^{\vee}_{*}{\mathbb{Q}}_{J^{\vee}_{C}}\to\pi^{\vee}_{*}{\mathbb{Q}}_{J^{\vee}_{C}}[-2g]

which is independent of
d
.

We finish with a brief summary of the parallel statements in global cohomology.

(i)

The cohomological Fourier transforms induce two sequences of semi-orthogonal idempotents
{\mathfrak{p}}^{d}_{\leq k},{\mathfrak{p}}^{\vee}_{\leq k}
, which give a new description of the perverse filtrations

\displaystyle P_{k}H^{*}(\overline{J}_{C}^{d},{\mathbb{Q}})=\mathrm{Im}\left({\mathfrak{p}}^{d}_{\leq k}:H^{*}(\overline{J}_{C}^{d},{\mathbb{Q}})\to H^{*}(\overline{J}_{C}^{d},{\mathbb{Q}})\right),

\displaystyle P_{k}H^{*}(\overline{J}_{C}^{\vee},{\mathbb{Q}})=\mathrm{Im}\left({\mathfrak{p}}^{\vee}_{\leq k}:H^{*}(\overline{J}_{C}^{\vee},{\mathbb{Q}})\to H^{*}(\overline{J}_{C}^{\vee},{\mathbb{Q}})\right).

(ii)

The perverse filtration
P_{\bullet}H^{*}(\overline{J}_{C}^{d},{\mathbb{Q}})
is multiplicative with respect to the cup-product,
i.e.
, for integers
k,l
we have

\cup:P_{k}H^{*}(\overline{J}^{d}_{C},{\mathbb{Q}})\otimes P_{l}H^{*}(\overline{J}^{d}_{C},{\mathbb{Q}})\to P_{k+l}H^{*}(\overline{J}^{d}_{C},{\mathbb{Q}}).

In particular, the associated graded

(41)

{\mathbb{H}}^{d}:=\bigoplus_{k,m}\mathrm{Gr}^{P}_{k}H^{m}(\overline{J}^{d}_{C},{\mathbb{Q}})

inherits a cup-product

\overline{\cup}:{\mathbb{H}}^{d}\otimes{\mathbb{H}}^{d}\to{\mathbb{H}}^{d}

making it a bigraded
H^{*}(B,{\mathbb{Q}})
-algebra. By definition, elements in
H^{m}(B,{\mathbb{Q}})
have bigrading
(0,m)
.

(iii)

There is a commutative diagram

{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{m}(\overline{J}^{\vee}_{C},{\mathbb{Q}})\otimes H^{n}(\overline{J}^{\vee}_{C},{\mathbb{Q}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{\geq m+n-2g}(\overline{J}^{\vee}_{C},{\mathbb{Q}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{*}(\overline{J}^{d}_{C},{\mathbb{Q}})\otimes H^{*}(\overline{J}^{d}_{C},{\mathbb{Q}})}
{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces H^{*}(\overline{J}^{d}_{C},{\mathbb{Q}}).}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces*^{d}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathfrak{F}}^{d}\otimes{\mathfrak{F}}^{d}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces{\mathfrak{F}}^{d}}
\scriptstyle{\lx@inpgf@ignorespaces\cup}

Moreover, the reduced convolution product

*_{\mathrm{red}}:H^{m}(\overline{J}^{\vee}_{C},{\mathbb{Q}})\otimes H^{n}(\overline{J}^{\vee}_{C},{\mathbb{Q}})\to H^{m+n-2g}(\overline{J}^{\vee}_{C},{\mathbb{Q}})

is independent of
d
.

2.3.
Degree-independence

Our main theorem for compactified Jacobian families of integral locally planar curves is the following.

Theorem 2.6
.

Let
\pi_{d}:\overline{J}^{d}_{C}\to B
and
\pi^{\vee}:\overline{J}_{C}^{\vee}\to B
be as in Sections
2.1
and
2.2
.

(i)

(Weak Fourier-stability) For integers
k,l
, the perverse truncation

{\mathfrak{F}}^{d}_{l}:{{}^{\mathfrak{p}}}\tau_{\leq k+\dim B}(\pi_{*}^{\vee}{\mathbb{Q}}_{\overline{J}_{C}^{\vee}})\to\pi_{d*}{\mathbb{Q}}_{\overline{J}^{d}_{C}}[2l-2g]

is zero if
l<2g-k
, and factors through

{\mathfrak{F}}^{d}_{l}:{{}^{\mathfrak{p}}}\tau_{\leq k+\dim B}(\pi_{*}^{\vee}{\mathbb{Q}}_{\overline{J}_{C}^{\vee}})\to{{}^{\mathfrak{p}}}\tau_{\leq l+\dim B}(\pi_{d*}{\mathbb{Q}}_{\overline{J}^{d}_{C}})[2l-2g]

if
l\geq 2g-k
. Similarly, the perverse truncation

({\mathfrak{F}}^{d})^{-1}_{l}:{{}^{\mathfrak{p}}}\tau_{\leq k+\dim B}(\pi_{d*}{\mathbb{Q}}_{\overline{J}_{C}^{d}})\to\pi_{*}^{\vee}{\mathbb{Q}}_{\overline{J}^{\vee}_{C}}[2l-2g]

is zero if
l<2g-k
, and factors through

({\mathfrak{F}}^{d})^{-1}_{l}:{{}^{\mathfrak{p}}}\tau_{\leq k+\dim B}(\pi_{d*}{\mathbb{Q}}_{\overline{J}_{C}^{d}})\to{{}^{\mathfrak{p}}}\tau_{\leq l+\dim B}(\pi_{*}^{\vee}{\mathbb{Q}}_{\overline{J}^{\vee}_{C}})[2l-2g]

if
l\geq 2g-k
. For
0\leq k\leq 2g
,
{\mathfrak{F}}^{d}_{k}
and
({\mathfrak{F}}^{d})^{-1}_{2g-k}
induce mutually inverse isomorphisms

(42)

{\mathcal{H}}^{\vee}_{(2g-k)}[2g-2k]\xrightleftharpoons[\overline{({\mathfrak{F}}^{d})_{2g-k}^{-1}}]{\overline{{\mathfrak{F}}^{d}_{k}}}{\mathcal{H}}^{d}_{(k)}.

(ii)

(Multiplicativity for
*_{\mathrm{red}}
) For integers
k,l
, the perverse truncation

*_{\mathrm{red}}:{{}^{\mathfrak{p}}}\tau_{\leq k+\dim B}(\pi_{*}^{\vee}{\mathbb{Q}}_{\overline{J}_{C}^{\vee}})\otimes{{}^{\mathfrak{p}}}\tau_{\leq l+\dim B}(\pi_{*}^{\vee}{\mathbb{Q}}_{\overline{J}_{C}^{\vee}})\to\pi_{*}^{\vee}{\mathbb{Q}}_{\overline{J}_{C}^{\vee}}[-2g],

of the reduced convolution product in (
40
) factors through

*_{\mathrm{red}}:{{}^{\mathfrak{p}}}\tau_{\leq k+\dim B}(\pi_{*}^{\vee}{\mathbb{Q}}_{\overline{J}_{C}^{\vee}})\otimes{{}^{\mathfrak{p}}}\tau_{\leq l+\dim B}(\pi_{*}^{\vee}{\mathbb{Q}}_{\overline{J}_{C}^{\vee}})\to{{}^{\mathfrak{p}}}\tau_{\leq k+l-2g+\dim B}(\pi_{*}^{\vee}{\mathbb{Q}}_{\overline{J}_{C}^{\vee}})[-2g],

In particular, the reduced convolution product descends to a collection of morphisms

(43)

\overline{*}_{\mathrm{red}}:{\mathcal{H}}^{\vee}_{(k)}\otimes{\mathcal{H}}^{\vee}_{(l)}\to{\mathcal{H}}^{\vee}_{(k+l-2g)}[-2g].

(iii)

(Degree-independence) The graded ring object
{\mathcal{H}}^{d}\in D^{b}_{c}(B)
in (
34
) is completely determined by the morphisms
\overline{*}_{\mathrm{red}}
. In particular, for integers
d,d^{\prime}
, there is an isomorphism

{\mathcal{H}}^{d}\simeq{\mathcal{H}}^{d^{\prime}}\in D^{b}_{c}(B)

of graded ring objects.

Proof.

Essentially all three parts are consequences of the
\frac{1}{2}
Fourier vanishing. We begin with part (i). In view of the realization Theorem
2.2
, for the first statement of (i) it suffices to show

(44)

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{F}}^{d}_{l}\circ{\mathfrak{p}}^{\vee}_{\leq k}=0,\quad l<2g-k,

(45)

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{q}}^{d}_{\geq l+1}\circ{\mathfrak{F}}^{d}_{l}=0,

where
{\mathfrak{q}}^{d}_{\geq l+1}
is defined in (
30
). Expanding the left-hand side of both equations

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{F}}^{d}_{l}\circ{\mathfrak{p}}^{\vee}_{\leq k}=\sum_{i\leq k}{\mathfrak{F}}^{d}_{l}\circ({\mathfrak{F}}^{d})^{-1}_{i}\circ{\mathfrak{F}}^{d}_{2g-i},

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{q}}^{d}_{\geq l+1}\circ{\mathfrak{F}}^{d}_{l}=\sum_{i\geq l+1}{\mathfrak{F}}^{d}_{i}\circ({\mathfrak{F}}^{d})^{-1}_{2g-i}\circ{\mathfrak{F}}^{d}_{l},

we see that (
44
) (resp. (
45
)) follows directly from (
\frac{1}{2}
FV2) (resp. (
\frac{1}{2}
FV1)). The second statement of (i) is parallel.

To prove (
42
) we first observe that

({\mathfrak{F}}^{d})^{-1}_{2g-k}\circ{\mathfrak{p}}_{\leq k-1}^{d}=0;

hence
({\mathfrak{F}}^{d})^{-1}_{2g-k}
descends to a well-defined morphism

\overline{({\mathfrak{F}}^{d})^{-1}_{2g-k}}:{\mathcal{H}}^{d}_{(k)}\to{\mathcal{H}}^{\vee}_{(2g-k)}[2g-2k].

Similarly,
{\mathfrak{F}}^{d}_{k}
descends to a well-defined morphism

\overline{{\mathfrak{F}}^{d}_{k}}:{\mathcal{H}}^{\vee}_{(2g-k)}[2g-2k]\to{\mathcal{H}}^{d}_{(k)}.

To show that they are inverse to each other, we consider the identities

\displaystyle{\mathfrak{p}}_{\leq k}^{d}

\displaystyle={\mathfrak{F}}^{d}\circ({\mathfrak{F}}^{d})^{-1}\circ{\mathfrak{p}}_{\leq k}^{d}=\sum_{i\geq 2g-k}{\mathfrak{F}}^{d}_{2g-i}\circ({\mathfrak{F}}^{d})^{-1}_{i}\circ{\mathfrak{p}}_{\leq k}^{d}

(46)
